      <div class="section" id="stat.245A.075">
        <h1 class="shn">245A.075 DISQUALIFIED INDIVIDUAL; DENIAL, CONDITIONAL LICENSE, REVOCATION.</h1>
        <p>(a) For the purpose of keeping a disqualified individual away from individuals receiving services in a license holder's home, when the disqualified individual has not received a set-aside and a variance has not been granted under chapter 245C, the commissioner may issue:</p>
        <p>(1) an order of denial of an application;</p>
        <p>(2) an order of conditional license; or</p>
        <p>(3) an order of revocation.</p>
        <p>(b) An order issued by the commissioner under this section is subject to notice and appeal rights provided under this chapter as follows:</p>
        <p>(1) an order of denial of an application according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.05">245A.05</a>;</p>
        <p>(2) an order of conditional license according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.06">245A.06</a>; and</p>
        <p>(3) an order of revocation of a license according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.07">245A.07</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245A.075.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/228/#laws.2.9.0">2014 c 228 art 2 s 9</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245A.08">
        <h1 class="shn">245A.08 HEARINGS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.08.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Receipt of appeal; conduct of hearing.</span></h2>
          <p>Upon receiving a timely appeal or petition pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.05">245A.05</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.07#stat.245A.07.3">245A.07, subdivision 3</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245C.28">245C.28</a>, the commissioner shall issue a notice of and order for hearing to the appellant under chapter 14 and Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/1400.8505">1400.8505</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/1400.8612">1400.8612</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.08.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Conduct of hearings.</span></h2>
          <p>At any hearing provided for by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.05">245A.05</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.07#stat.245A.07.3">245A.07, subdivision 3</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245C.28">245C.28</a>, the appellant may be represented by counsel and has the right to call, examine, and cross-examine witnesses. The administrative law judge may require the presence of witnesses and evidence by subpoena on behalf of any party.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.08.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Consolidated contested case hearings.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) When a denial of a license under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.05">245A.05</a> or a licensing sanction under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.07#stat.245A.07.3">245A.07, subdivision 3</a>, is based on a disqualification for which reconsideration was timely requested and which was not set aside under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245C.22">245C.22</a>, the scope of the contested case hearing shall include the disqualification and the licensing sanction or denial of a license, unless otherwise specified in this subdivision. When the licensing sanction or denial of a license is based on a determination of maltreatment under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/626.557">626.557</a> or chapter 260E, or a disqualification for serious or recurring maltreatment which was not set aside, the scope of the contested case hearing shall include the maltreatment determination, disqualification, and the licensing sanction or denial of a license, unless otherwise specified in this subdivision. In such cases, a fair hearing under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.045">256.045</a> shall not be conducted as provided for in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245C.27">245C.27, </a><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260E.33">260E.33</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/626.557#stat.626.557.9d">626.557, subdivision 9d</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Reconsideration of a maltreatment determination under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260E.33">260E.33</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/626.557">626.557</a>, subdivision 9d, and reconsideration of a disqualification under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245C.22">245C.22</a>, shall not be conducted when:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) a denial of a license under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.05">245A.05</a>, or a licensing sanction under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.07">245A.07</a>, is based on a determination that the license holder is responsible for maltreatment or the disqualification of a license holder is based on serious or recurring maltreatment;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the denial of a license or licensing sanction is issued at the same time as the maltreatment determination or disqualification; and</p>
          <p class="in">(3) the license holder appeals the maltreatment determination or disqualification, and denial of a license or licensing sanction. In these cases, a fair hearing shall not be conducted under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245C.27">245C.27</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260E.33">260E.33</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/626.557">626.557</a>, subdivision 9d. The scope of the contested case hearing must include the maltreatment determination, disqualification, and denial of a license or licensing sanction.</p>
          <p class="in">Notwithstanding clauses (1) to (3), if the license holder appeals the maltreatment determination or disqualification, but does not appeal the denial of a license or a licensing sanction, reconsideration of the maltreatment determination shall be conducted under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260E.33">260E.33</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/626.557#stat.626.557.9d">626.557, subdivision 9d</a>, and reconsideration of the disqualification shall be conducted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245C.22">245C.22</a>. In such cases, a fair hearing shall also be conducted as provided under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245C.27">245C.27</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260E.33">260E.33</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/626.557#stat.626.557.9d">626.557, subdivision 9d</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) In consolidated contested case hearings regarding sanctions issued in family adult day services, adult foster care, and community residential settings, the county attorney shall defend the commissioner's orders in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.16#stat.245A.16.4">245A.16, subdivision 4</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) The commissioner's final order under subdivision 5 is the final agency action on the issue of maltreatment and disqualification, including for purposes of subsequent background studies under chapter 245C and is the only administrative appeal of the final agency determination, specifically, including a challenge to the accuracy and completeness of data under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.04">13.04</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(e) When consolidated hearings under this subdivision involve a licensing sanction based on a previous maltreatment determination for which the commissioner has issued a final order in an appeal of that determination under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.045">256.045</a>, or the individual failed to exercise the right to appeal the previous maltreatment determination under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260E.33">260E.33 </a>or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/626.557#stat.626.557.9d">626.557, subdivision 9d</a>, the commissioner's order is conclusive on the issue of maltreatment. In such cases, the scope of the administrative law judge's review shall be limited to the disqualification and the licensing sanction or denial of a license. In the case of a denial of a license or a licensing sanction issued to a facility based on a maltreatment determination regarding an individual who is not the license holder or a household member, the scope of the administrative law judge's review includes the maltreatment determination.</p>
          <p class="in">(f) The hearings of all parties may be consolidated into a single contested case hearing upon consent of all parties and the administrative law judge, if:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) a maltreatment determination or disqualification, which was not set aside under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245C.22">245C.22</a>, is the basis for a denial of a license under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.05">245A.05</a> or a licensing sanction under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.07">245A.07</a>;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) the disqualified subject is an individual other than the license holder and upon whom a background study must be conducted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245C.03">245C.03</a>; and</p>
          <p class="in">(3) the individual has a hearing right under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245C.27">245C.27</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(g) When a denial of a license under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.05">245A.05</a> or a licensing sanction under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.07">245A.07</a> is based on a disqualification for which reconsideration was requested and was not set aside under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245C.22">245C.22</a>, and the individual otherwise has no hearing right under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245C.27">245C.27</a>, the scope of the administrative law judge's review shall include the denial or sanction and a determination whether the disqualification should be set aside, unless section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245C.24">245C.24</a> prohibits the set-aside of the disqualification. In determining whether the disqualification should be set aside, the administrative law judge shall consider the factors under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245C.22#stat.245C.22.4">245C.22, subdivision 4</a>, to determine whether the individual poses a risk of harm to any person receiving services from the license holder.</p>
          <p class="in">(h) Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245C.30#stat.245C.30.5">245C.30, subdivision 5</a>, when a licensing sanction under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.07">245A.07</a> is based on the termination of a variance under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245C.30#stat.245C.30.4">245C.30, subdivision 4</a>, the scope of the administrative law judge's review shall include the sanction and a determination whether the disqualification should be set aside, unless section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245C.24">245C.24 </a>prohibits the set-aside of the disqualification. In determining whether the disqualification should be set aside, the administrative law judge shall consider the factors under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245C.22#stat.245C.22.4">245C.22, subdivision 4</a>, to determine whether the individual poses a risk of harm to any person receiving services from the license holder.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.08.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Burden of proof.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) At a hearing regarding a licensing sanction under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.07">245A.07</a>, including consolidated hearings under subdivision 2a, the commissioner may demonstrate reasonable cause for action taken by submitting statements, reports, or affidavits to substantiate the allegations that the license holder failed to comply fully with applicable law or rule. If the commissioner demonstrates that reasonable cause existed, the burden of proof shifts to the license holder to demonstrate by a preponderance of the evidence that the license holder was in full compliance with those laws or rules that the commissioner alleges the license holder violated, at the time that the commissioner alleges the violations of law or rules occurred.</p>
          <p>(b) At a hearing on denial of an application, the applicant bears the burden of proof to demonstrate by a preponderance of the evidence that the appellant has complied fully with this chapter and other applicable law or rule and that the application should be approved and a license granted.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.08.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Recommendation of administrative law judge.</span></h2>
          <p>The administrative law judge shall recommend whether or not the commissioner's order should be affirmed. The recommendations must be consistent with this chapter and the rules of the commissioner. The recommendations must be in writing and accompanied by findings of fact and conclusions and must be mailed to the parties by certified mail to their last known addresses as shown on the license or application.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.08.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Notice of commissioner's final order.</span></h2>
          <p>After considering the findings of fact, conclusions, and recommendations of the administrative law judge, the commissioner shall issue a final order. The commissioner shall consider, but shall not be bound by, the recommendations of the administrative law judge. The appellant must be notified of the commissioner's final order as required by chapter 14 and Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/1400.8505">1400.8505</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/1400.8612">1400.8612</a>. The notice must also contain information about the appellant's rights under chapter 14 and Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/1400.8505">1400.8505</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/1400.8612">1400.8612</a>. The institution of proceedings for judicial review of the commissioner's final order shall not stay the enforcement of the final order except as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/14.65">14.65</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.08.5a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5a.<span class="headnote">Granting subsequent license.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A license holder and each controlling individual of a license holder whose license has been revoked because of noncompliance with applicable law or rule must not be granted a license for five years following the revocation. Notwithstanding the five-year restriction, when a license is revoked because a person, other than the license holder, resides in the home where services are provided and that person has a disqualification that is not set aside and no variance has been granted, the former license holder may reapply for a license when:</p>
          <p>(1) the person with a disqualification, who is not a minor child, is no longer residing in the home and is prohibited from residing in or returning to the home; or</p>
          <p>(2) the person with the disqualification is a minor child, the restriction applies until the minor child becomes an adult and permanently moves away from the home or five years, whichever is less.</p>
          <p>(b) An applicant or controlling individual whose application was denied must not be granted a license for two years following a denial, unless the applicant's subsequent application contains new information which constitutes a substantial change in the conditions that caused the previous denial. The addition of a new co-applicant in a subsequent application does not constitute a substantial change. If an applicant or controlling individual whose application was denied is affiliated with a subsequent application, and two years have not passed since the denial, the subsequent application must be denied.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245A.08.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/333/#laws.0.8.0">1987 c 333 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.2.81.0">1989 c 282 art 2 s 81</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/568/#laws.2.46.0">1990 c 568 art 2 s 46</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/248/#laws.0.28.0">1997 c 248 s 28</a>,29; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/245/#laws.4.10.0">1999 c 245 art 4 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/327/#laws.0.6.0">2000 c 327 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/9/#laws.14.22.0">1Sp2001 c 9 art 14 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/379/#laws.1.113.0">2002 c 379 art 1 s 113</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/15/#laws.1.33.0">2003 c 15 art 1 s 33</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/288/#laws.1.21.0">2004 c 288 art 1 s 21</a>-23; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/4/#laws.1.15.0">1Sp2005 c 4 art 1 s 15</a>,16; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/112/#laws.0.11.0">2007 c 112 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.8.14.0">2013 c 108 art 8 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/228/#laws.2.10.0">2014 c 228 art 2 s 10</a>,11; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/1/2/#laws.8.45.0">1Sp2020 c 2 art 8 s 45</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.2.46.0">2024 c 80 art 2 s 46</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245A.081">
        <h1 class="shn">245A.081 SETTLEMENT AGREEMENT.</h1>
        <p>(a) A license holder who has made a timely appeal pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.06">245A.06</a>, subdivision 4, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.07#stat.245A.07.3">245A.07, subdivision 3</a>, or the commissioner may initiate a discussion about a possible settlement agreement related to the licensing sanction. For the purposes of this section, the following conditions apply to a settlement agreement reached by the parties:</p>
        <p>(1) if the parties enter into a settlement agreement, the effect of the agreement shall be that the appeal is withdrawn and the agreement shall constitute the full agreement between the commissioner and the party who filed the appeal; and</p>
        <p>(2) the settlement agreement must identify the agreed-upon actions the license holder has taken and will take in order to achieve and maintain compliance with the licensing requirements that the commissioner determined the license holder had violated.</p>
        <p>(b) Neither the license holder nor the commissioner is required to initiate a settlement discussion under this section.</p>
        <p>(c) If a settlement discussion is initiated by the license holder, the commissioner shall respond to the license holder within 14 calendar days of receipt of the license holder's submission.</p>
        <p>(d) If the commissioner agrees to engage in settlement discussions, the commissioner may decide at any time not to continue settlement discussions with a license holder.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245A.081.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/71/#laws.7.4.0">2015 c 71 art 7 s 4</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245A.085">
        <h1 class="shn">245A.085 CONSOLIDATION OF HEARINGS; RECONSIDERATION.</h1>
        <p>Hearings authorized under this chapter, sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.045">256.045</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.04">256B.04</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/626.557">626.557</a>, and chapters 245C and 260E, shall be consolidated if feasible and in accordance with other applicable statutes and rules. Reconsideration under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245C.28">245C.28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260E.33">260E.33</a>; and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/626.557#stat.626.557.9d">626.557, subdivision 9d</a>, shall also be consolidated if feasible.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245A.085.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/375/#laws.1.15.0">2002 c 375 art 1 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/15/#laws.1.33.0">2003 c 15 art 1 s 33</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/288/#laws.1.24.0">2004 c 288 art 1 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.18.5.0">2012 c 216 art 18 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/1/2/#laws.8.46.0">1Sp2020 c 2 art 8 s 46</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245A.09">
        <h1 class="shn">245A.09 RULES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.09.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Commissioner's authority.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall adopt rules under chapter 14 to govern the operation, maintenance, and licensure of programs subject to licensure under this chapter. The commissioner shall not adopt any rules that are inconsistent with or duplicative of existing state or federal regulations. Nothing in this subdivision shall be construed to prohibit the commissioner from incorporating existing state or federal regulations or accreditation standards by reference.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.09.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Standards and regulatory methods.</span></h2>
          <p>This subdivision applies to rules governing this chapter that are adopted after July 1, 1987. As appropriate for each type of license:</p>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall give preference in rule to standards that describe program outcomes and the practices that have been shown to result in the desired program outcomes.</p>
          <p>(b) The rules may include model program standards for each type of program licensed by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(c) The rules shall include basic licensing standards governing licensure of each type of program licensed by the commissioner. The basic licensing standards must be met by all applicants and license holders. Basic licensing standards must include, but are not limited to:</p>
          <p>(1) standards for adequate staff that take into account the age distribution and severity of the disability of persons served by the program;</p>
          <p>(2) safety standards that take into account the size and conditions of the physical plant and studies of fire safety including studies of the interaction between fire detection factors, fire spread factors, and evacuation factors in case of a fire;</p>
          <p>(3) standards for program services that describe, when appropriate, adequate levels of shelter, nutrition, planned activities, materials, and qualifications of individuals responsible for administering and delivering program services;</p>
          <p>(4) standards that describe the characteristics of the settings where program services are to be delivered; and</p>
          <p>(5) health and sanitation standards.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.09.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Reduction of fees.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may adopt rules under subdivision 1 to provide for the reduction of fees established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.10">245A.10</a> when a license holder substantially exceeds the basic standards for licensure.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.09.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Evaluation of rules.</span></h2>
          <p>For rules adopted under this section after July 1, 1987, the commissioner shall evaluate the effects of the rules within three years after the date of adoption and at least once every five years thereafter. The evaluation must include an assessment of any discrepancies between the actual and intended effects of the rules, identification of necessary revisions, if any, and a discussion of the rules' effect on the availability and quality of licensed programs. The commissioner shall consider the results of the evaluation in amending and writing rules.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.09.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Other duties of commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>For rules adopted after July 1, 1987, the commissioner shall:</p>
          <p>(1) summarize the rules in language understandable to the general public and inform license holders and applicants where they may obtain a copy of the rules and the summary;</p>
          <p>(2) develop and provide each applicant with information describing the services offered to applicants by the commissioner and explaining the penalties for operating an unlicensed program or failing to fully comply with the commissioner's correction orders or applicable laws or rules;</p>
          <p>(3) upon request, interpret rules for applicants and license holders; and</p>
          <p>(4) take measures to ensure that rules are enforced uniformly throughout the state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.09.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Consultation with affected parties.</span></h2>
          <p>In developing rules, the commissioner shall request and receive consultation from: other state departments and agencies; counties and other affected political subdivisions that reflect the diversity of political subdivisions affected by the rule; persons and relatives of persons using the program governed by the rule; advocacy groups; and representatives of license holders affected by the rule. In choosing parties for consultation, the commissioner shall choose individuals and representatives of groups that reflect a cross section of urban, suburban, and rural areas of the state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.09.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Regulatory methods.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Where appropriate and feasible the commissioner shall identify and implement alternative methods of regulation and enforcement to the extent authorized in this subdivision. These methods shall include:</p>
          <p>(1) expansion of the types and categories of licenses that may be granted;</p>
          <p>(2) when the standards of another state or federal governmental agency or an independent accreditation body have been shown to require the same standards, methods, or alternative methods to achieve substantially the same intended outcomes as the licensing standards, the commissioner shall consider compliance with the governmental or accreditation standards to be equivalent to partial compliance with the licensing standards; and</p>
          <p>(3) use of an abbreviated inspection that employs key standards that have been shown to predict full compliance with the rules.</p>
          <p>(b) If the commissioner accepts accreditation as documentation of compliance with a licensing standard under paragraph (a), the commissioner shall continue to investigate complaints related to noncompliance with all licensing standards. The commissioner may take a licensing action for noncompliance under this chapter and shall recognize all existing appeal rights regarding any licensing actions taken under this chapter.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner shall work with the commissioners of health; public safety; administration; children, youth, and families; and education in consolidating duplicative licensing and certification rules and standards if the commissioner determines that consolidation is administratively feasible, would significantly reduce the cost of licensing, and would not reduce the protection given to persons receiving services in licensed programs. Where administratively feasible and appropriate, the commissioner shall work with the commissioners of health, public safety, administration, and education in conducting joint agency inspections of programs.</p>
          <p>(d) The commissioner shall work with the commissioners of health; public safety; administration; children, youth, and families; and education in establishing a single point of application for applicants who are required to obtain concurrent licensure from more than one of the commissioners listed in this clause.</p>
          <p>(e) Unless otherwise specified in statute, the commissioner may conduct routine inspections biennially.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.09.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Interpretive guidelines; authority.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of human services may develop and publish interpretive guidelines.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.09.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Effect of interpretive guidelines.</span></h2>
          <p>Interpretive guidelines do not have the force and effect of law and have no precedential effect, but may be relied on by consumers, providers of service, county agencies, the Department of Human Services, and others concerned until revoked or modified. A guideline may be expressly revoked or modified by the commissioner, by the issuance of another interpretive guideline, but may not be revoked or modified retroactively to the detriment of consumers, providers of service, county agencies, the Department of Human Services, or others concerned. A change in the law or an interpretation of the law occurring after the interpretive guidelines are issued, whether in the form of a statute, court decision, administrative ruling, or subsequent interpretive guideline, results in the revocation or modification of the previously adopted guidelines to the extent that the change affects the guidelines.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.09.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.</h2>
          <p>MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.2.75.0">2024 c 80 art 2 s 75</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.09.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Issuance; discretion of commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>The issuance of interpretive guidelines is at the discretion of the commissioner of human services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.09.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/262/#laws.5.7.0">2014 c 262 art 5 s 7</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245A.09.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/333/#laws.0.9.0">1987 c 333 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/338/#laws.0.7.0">1993 c 338 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/207/#laws.2.15.0">1995 c 207 art 2 s 15</a>-19; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/187/#laws.4.7.0">1997 c 187 art 4 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/248/#laws.0.30.0">1997 c 248 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/327/#laws.0.6.0">2000 c 327 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/130/#laws.0.12.0">2003 c 130 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/14/#laws.6.8.0">1Sp2003 c 14 art 6 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/56/#laws.0.1.0">2005 c 56 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/6/#laws.16.2.0">1Sp2017 c 6 art 16 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.2.47.0">2024 c 80 art 2 s 47</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245A.091"><b>245A.091</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/248/#laws.0.51.0">1997 c 248 s 51</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245A.095">
        <h1 class="shn">245A.095 RULES FOR PROGRAMS SERVING PERSONS WITH MENTAL ILLNESSES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.095.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">License required.</span></h2>
          <p>Residential programs with five or more persons with a mental illness must be licensed under this chapter. To assure that this requirement is met, the commissioner of health, in cooperation with the commissioner of human services, shall monitor licensed boarding care homes, board and lodging houses, and supervised living facilities.</p>
          <p>By January 1, 1989, the commissioner of health shall recommend to the legislature an appropriate method for enforcing this requirement.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.095.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Rules.</span></h2>
          <p>In developing rules for serving persons with mental illness, the commissioner of human services shall assure that persons with mental illness are provided with needed treatment or support in the least restrictive, most appropriate environment, that supportive residential care in small homelike settings is available for persons needing that care, and that a mechanism is developed to ensure that no person is placed in a care or treatment setting inappropriate for meeting the person's needs. To the maximum extent possible, the rule shall assure that length of stay is governed solely by client need and shall allow for a variety of innovative and flexible approaches in meeting residential and support needs of persons with mental illness.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.095.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Specific review of rules.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall:</p>
          <p>(1) provide in rule for additional types of programs and services, including but not limited to supportive small group residential care, semi-independent and apartment living services, and crisis and respite services, to address the residential treatment and support needs of persons with mental illness;</p>
          <p>(2) review category I and II programs established in Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9520.0500">9520.0500 </a>to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9520.0670">9520.0670</a> to ensure that the categories of programs provide a continuum of residential service programs for persons with mental illness, including but not limited to programs meeting needs for intensive treatment, crisis and respite care, and rehabilitation and training;</p>
          <p>(3) provide in rule for a definition of the term "treatment" as used in relation to persons with mental illness;</p>
          <p>(4) adjust funding mechanisms by rule as needed to reflect the requirements established by rule for services being provided;</p>
          <p>(5) review and recommend staff educational requirements and staff training as needed; and</p>
          <p>(6) review and make changes in rules relating to residential care and service programs for persons with mental illness as the commissioner may determine necessary.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.095.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.4.64.0">1989 c 282 art 4 s 64</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245A.095.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/333/#laws.0.10.0">1987 c 333 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/411/#laws.0.5.0">1988 c 411 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.4.61.0">1989 c 282 art 4 s 61</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/327/#laws.0.6.0">2000 c 327 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/158/#laws.1.214.0">2016 c 158 art 1 s 214</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/182/#laws.1.44.0">2018 c 182 art 1 s 44</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245A.0951">
        <h1 class="shn">245A.0951 ADOPTION OF RULES FOR LICENSURE OF SECURE TREATMENT FACILITIES.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner of human services shall adopt rules to govern the licensure of secure treatment facilities operated by the Minnesota Sex Offender Program or any other facility operated by the executive board for a person committed as a sexual psychopathic personality or a sexually dangerous person.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245A.0951.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/79/#laws.9.6.0">2024 c 79 art 9 s 6</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245A.10">
        <h1 class="shn">245A.10 FEES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.10.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Application or license fee required; programs exempt from fee.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Unless exempt under paragraph (b), the commissioner shall charge a fee for evaluation of applications and inspection of programs which are licensed under this chapter.</p>
          <p>(b) Except as provided under subdivision 2, no application or license fee shall be charged for a child foster residence setting, adult foster care, or a community residential setting.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.10.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">County fees for applications and licensing inspections.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) For purposes of adult foster care and child foster residence setting licensing and licensing the physical plant of a community residential setting, under this chapter, a county agency may charge a fee to a corporate applicant or corporate license holder to recover the actual cost of licensing inspections, not to exceed $500 annually.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Counties may elect to reduce or waive the fees in paragraph (a) under the following circumstances:</p>
          <p>(1) in cases of financial hardship;</p>
          <p>(2) if the county has a shortage of providers in the county's area; or</p>
          <p>(3) for new providers.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.10.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Application fee for initial license or certification.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For fees required under subdivision 1, an applicant for an initial license or certification issued by the commissioner shall submit a $500 application fee with each new application required under this subdivision. An applicant for an initial day services facility license under chapter 245D shall submit a $250 application fee with each new application. The application fee shall not be prorated, is nonrefundable, and is in lieu of the annual license or certification fee that expires on December 31. The commissioner shall not process an application until the application fee is paid.</p>
          <p>(b) Except as provided in paragraph (c), an applicant shall apply for a license to provide services at a specific location.</p>
          <p>(c) For a license to provide home and community-based services to persons with disabilities or age 65 and older under chapter 245D, an applicant shall submit an application to provide services statewide.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.10.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">License or certification fee for certain programs.</span></h2>
          <p>(a)(1) A program licensed to provide one or more of the home and community-based services and supports identified under chapter <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245D">245D</a> to persons with disabilities or age 65 and older, shall pay an annual nonrefundable license fee based on revenues derived from the provision of services that would require licensure under chapter <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245D">245D</a> during the calendar year immediately preceding the year in which the license fee is paid, according to the following schedule:</p>

          <p>(2) If requested, the license holder shall provide the commissioner information to verify the license holder's annual revenues or other information as needed, including copies of documents submitted to the Department of Revenue.</p>
          <p>(3) At each annual renewal, a license holder may elect to pay the highest renewal fee, and not provide annual revenue information to the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(4) A license holder that knowingly provides the commissioner incorrect revenue amounts for the purpose of paying a lower license fee shall be subject to a civil penalty in the amount of double the fee the provider should have paid.</p>
          <p>(b) A substance use disorder treatment program licensed under chapter <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245G">245G</a>, to provide substance use disorder treatment shall pay an annual nonrefundable license fee based on the following schedule:</p>

          <p class="bl">A detoxification program that also operates a withdrawal management program at the same location shall only pay one fee based upon the licensed capacity of the program with the higher overall capacity.</p>

        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.10.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/9/#laws.4.10.0">1Sp2011 c 9 art 4 s 10</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.10.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">License not issued until license or certification fee is paid.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall not issue or reissue a license or certification until the license or certification fee is paid. The commissioner shall send a bill for the license or certification fee to the billing address identified by the license holder. If the license holder does not submit the license or certification fee payment by the due date, the commissioner shall send the license holder a past due notice. If the license holder fails to pay the license or certification fee by the due date on the past due notice, the commissioner shall send a final notice to the license holder informing the license holder that the program license will expire on December 31 unless the license fee is paid before December 31. If a license expires, the program is no longer licensed and, unless exempt from licensure under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.03#stat.245A.03.2">245A.03, subdivision 2</a>, must not operate after the expiration date. After a license expires, if the former license holder wishes to provide licensed services, the former license holder must submit a new license application and application fee under subdivision 3.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.10.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Human services licensing fees to recover expenditures.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.1285#stat.16A.1285.2">16A.1285, subdivision 2</a>, related to activities for which the commissioner charges a fee, the commissioner must plan to fully recover direct expenditures for licensing activities under this chapter over a five-year period. The commissioner may have anticipated expenditures in excess of anticipated revenues in a biennium by using surplus revenues accumulated in previous bienniums.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.10.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Deposit of license fees.</span></h2>
          <p>A human services licensing account is created in the state government special revenue fund. Fees collected under subdivisions 3 and 4 must be deposited in the human services licensing account and are annually appropriated to the commissioner for licensing activities authorized under this chapter.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.10.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">License not reissued until outstanding debt is paid.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall not reissue a license or certification until the license holder has paid all outstanding debts related to a licensing fine or settlement agreement for which payment is delinquent. If the payment is past due, the commissioner shall send a past due notice informing the license holder that the program license will expire on December 31 unless the outstanding debt is paid before December 31. If a license expires, the program is no longer licensed and must not operate after the expiration date. After a license expires, if the former license holder wishes to provide licensed services, the former license holder must submit a new license application and application fee under subdivision 3.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
