        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.14.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/112/#laws.0.59.0">2007 c 112 s 59</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.14.14">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14.<span class="headnote">Attendance records for publicly funded services.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An adult day services program licensed under this chapter and according to Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9555.5105">9555.5105</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9555.6265">9555.6265</a>, must maintain documentation of actual attendance for each adult day service recipient for which the license holder is reimbursed by a governmental program. The records must be accessible to the commissioner during the program's hours of operation, they must be completed on the actual day of attendance, and they must include:</p>
          <p>(1) the first, middle, and last name of the recipient;</p>
          <p>(2) the time of day that the recipient was dropped off; and</p>
          <p>(3) the time of day that the recipient was picked up.</p>
          <p>(b) Adult day services programs licensed under this chapter that are designated for remote adult day services must maintain documentation of actual participation for each adult day service recipient for whom the license holder is reimbursed by a governmental program. The records must be accessible to the commissioner during the program's hours of operation, must be completed on the actual day service is provided, and must include the:</p>
          <p>(1) first, middle, and last name of the recipient;</p>
          <p>(2) time of day the remote services started;</p>
          <p>(3) time of day that the remote services ended; and</p>
          <p>(4) means by which the remote services were provided, through audio remote services or through audio and video remote services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.14.15">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.41">142B.41, subd 11</a>] </p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.14.16">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 16.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.41">142B.41, subd 12</a>] </p>
        </div>

          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.41">142B.41, subd 13</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>

      <div class="section" id="stat.245A.143">
        <h1 class="shn">245A.143 FAMILY ADULT DAY SERVICES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.143.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Scope.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The licensing standards in this section must be met to obtain and maintain a license to provide family adult day services. For the purposes of this section, family adult day services means a program operating fewer than 24 hours per day that provides functionally impaired adults age 18 or older with an individualized and coordinated set of services including health services, social services, and nutritional services that are directed at maintaining or improving the participants' capabilities for self-care.</p>
          <p>(b) A family adult day services license shall only be issued when the services are provided in the license holder's primary residence, and the license holder is the primary provider of care. The license holder may not serve more than eight adults at one time, including residents, if any, served under a license issued under Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9555.5105">9555.5105</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9555.6265">9555.6265</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) An adult foster care license holder may provide family adult day services under the license holder's adult foster care license if the license holder meets the requirements of this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.143.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For the purposes of this section, the terms defined in this subdivision have the following meanings unless otherwise provided for by text.</p>
          <p>(b) "Caregiver" means a spouse, adult child, parent, relative, friend, or others who normally provide unpaid support or care to the individual needing assistance. For the purpose of this section, the caregiver may or may not have legal or financial responsibility for the participant.</p>
          <p>(c) "Participant" means a functionally impaired adult receiving family adult day services.</p>
          <p>(d) "Consultation by a health care professional" means the review and oversight of the participant's health-related services by a registered nurse, physician, physician assistant, or mental health professional.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.143.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Policy and program information requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The license holder shall have available for review, and shall distribute to participants and their caregivers upon admission, written information about:</p>
          <p>(1) the scope of the programs, services, and care offered by the license holder;</p>
          <p>(2) a description of the population to be served by the license holder;</p>
          <p>(3) a description of individual conditions which the license holder is not prepared to accept, such as a communicable disease requiring isolation, a history of violence to self or others, unmanageable incontinence, or uncontrollable wandering;</p>
          <p>(4) the participants' rights and procedure for presenting grievances, including the name, address, and telephone number of the Office of Ombudsman for Long-Term Care and the county licensing department, to which a participant or participant's caregiver may submit an oral or written complaint;</p>
          <p>(5) the license holder's policy on and arrangements for providing transportation;</p>
          <p>(6) the license holder's policy on providing meals and snacks;</p>
          <p>(7) the license holder's fees, billing arrangements, and plans for payment;</p>
          <p>(8) the license holder's policy governing the presence of pets in the home;</p>
          <p>(9) the license holder's policy on smoking in the home;</p>
          <p>(10) types of insurance coverage carried by the license holder;</p>
          <p>(11) information on orientation requirements under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.65">245A.65, subdivisions 1, paragraph (c)</a>, and 2, paragraph (a), clause (4);</p>
          <p>(12) the terms and conditions of the license holder's license issued by the department;</p>
          <p>(13) the license holder's plan for emergency evacuation of participants involving fire, weather, and other disasters. The plan must include instructions for evacuation or rescue of participants, identification of an emergency shelter area, quarterly fire drill schedule, and staff responsibilities; and</p>
          <p>(14) the license holder's policy for handling harmful objects, materials, or equipment including the storage of poisonous chemicals, use of appliances, sharp instruments, matches, or any other potentially harmful materials.</p>
          <p>(b) The information in paragraph (a) must be provided in writing to the commissioner's representative upon request and must be available for inspection by the commissioner's representative at the home.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.143.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Admission screening and evaluation.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Before admitting an individual into the family adult day services program, the license holder shall screen the individual to determine how or whether the license holder can serve the individual, based on the license holder's policies, services, expertise, and the individual's needs and condition. If possible, the screening shall include an interview with the individual and with the individual's caregiver.</p>
          <p>(b) The screening required under paragraph (a) shall include an evaluation of the health, nutritional, and social services needs of the individual.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.143.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Service delivery plan.</span></h2>
          <p>Before providing family adult day services, an individual, the individual's caregiver, the legal representative if there is one, the county or private case manager, if applicable, and the license holder shall develop a service delivery plan. At a minimum, the service delivery plan shall include:</p>
          <p>(1) a description of the health services, nutritional services, and social services to be arranged or provided by the license holder and the frequency of those services and that the services will be based on the needs of the individual;</p>
          <p>(2) scheduled days and hours of participant's attendance at the license holder's home;</p>
          <p>(3) transportation arrangements for getting the participant to and from the license holder's home;</p>
          <p>(4) contingency plans if scheduled services cannot be provided by the license holder;</p>
          <p>(5) identification of responsibilities of the participant and the license holder with respect to payment for the services;</p>
          <p>(6) circumstances when emergency services will be called; and</p>
          <p>(7) identification of the license holder's discharge policy when services are no longer needed or when the participant's needs can no longer be met by the license holder.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.143.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Individual service plan.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The service plan must be coordinated with other plans of services for the participant, as appropriate.</p>
          <p>(b) The service plan must be dated and revised when there is a change in the needs of the participant or annually, whichever occurs sooner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.143.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Health services.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The license holder shall provide health services as specified in the service delivery plan under the direction of the designated caregiver or county or private case manager. Health services must include:</p>
          <p>(1) monitoring the participant's level of function and health while participating; taking appropriate action for a change in condition including immediately reporting changes to the participant's caregiver, physician, physician assistant, mental health professional, or registered nurse; and seeking consultation;</p>
          <p>(2) offering information to participants and caregivers on good health and safety practices; and</p>
          <p>(3) maintaining a listing of health resources available for referrals as needed by participants and caregivers.</p>
          <p>(b) Unless the person is a licensed health care practitioner qualified to administer medications, the person responsible for medication administration or assistance shall provide a certificate verifying successful completion of a trained medication aid program for unlicensed personnel approved by the Minnesota Department of Health or comparable program, or biennially provide evidence of competency as demonstrated to a registered nurse, physician, or physician assistant.</p>
          <p>(c) The license holder must have secure storage and safeguarding of all medications with storage of medications in their original container, know what information regarding medication administration must be reported to a health care professional, and must maintain a record of all medications administered.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.143.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Nutritional services.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The license holder shall ensure that food served is nutritious and meets any special dietary needs of the participants as prescribed by the participant's physician, advanced practice registered nurse, physician assistant, or dietitian as specified in the service delivery plan.</p>
          <p>(b) Food and beverages must be obtained, handled, and properly stored to prevent contamination, spoilage, or a threat to the health of a resident.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.143.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Social services.</span></h2>
          <p>The license holder, in consultation with the county or private case manager, when appropriate, shall actively assist the participant in identifying and achieving personal goals, support the participant in maintaining personal support networks and socially valued roles, provide assistance to the participant to enable community participation, and refer participants to the Office of Ombudsman for Long-Term Care and other advocacy organizations for assistance when there is a potential conflict of interest between the license holder and the participant.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.143.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Participant rights.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The license holder shall adopt and comply with a participant bill of rights. The rights shall include the participants' right to:</p>
          <p>(1) participate in the development of the service plan;</p>
          <p>(2) refuse services or participation;</p>
          <p>(3) privacy;</p>
          <p>(4) confidentiality of participant information; and</p>
          <p>(5) present grievances regarding treatment or services to the Office of Ombudsman for Long-Term Care or the county licensing department. The license holder's policies shall include a procedure for addressing participant grievances, including the name, address, and telephone number of the county licensing department, to which a participant or participant caregiver may submit an oral or written complaint.</p>
          <p>(b) The license holder shall post the participant rights in the home and shall provide a copy to the participant and the participant's primary caregiver and legal representative if the participant has one.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.143.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Staffing.</span></h2>
          <p>Whenever participants are in the home, there must be present at least one individual who is trained in basic first aid and certified in cardiopulmonary resuscitation and the treatment of obstructed airways. Whenever there are six, seven, or eight participants present, there must be a second staff person present.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.143.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Training.</span></h2>
          <p>The license holder and license holder's staff must annually complete 12 hours of training related to the health, nutritional, and social needs of the license holder's target population. License holders with six or more years of licensure under this section or as an adult foster care provider must annually complete six hours of training. The annual training must include training on the reporting of maltreatment of vulnerable adults under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/626.557">626.557</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/626.5572">626.5572</a>; license holder requirements governing maltreatment of vulnerable adults under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.65">245A.65</a>; and, when a license holder serves participants who rely on medical monitoring equipment to sustain life or monitor a medical condition, training on medical equipment as required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.155">245A.155</a> for foster care providers. A record of all training must be maintained in the home.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.143.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Residential requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The home where family adult day services are to be provided shall be classified as a residential group R-3 occupancy under the State Building Code and State Fire Code for purposes of building code and fire code inspections. A building code inspection is not required for licensure under this section. The state or local fire marshal must inspect the family adult day services home operating in the residence for compliance with the residential group R-3 occupancy provisions of the State Fire Code.</p>
          <p>(b) The licensed capacity of the home shall be limited by the amount of indoor space available for use by participants. The total indoor space available for use by participants must equal at least 35 square feet for each participant, the license holder, and each staff member present in the home. In determining the square footage of usable indoor space available, the following must not be counted: hallways, stairways, closets, offices, restrooms, and utility and storage areas. The usable indoor space available must include a room or an area that can be used as private space for providing personal hygiene services or social services to participants.</p>
          <p>(c) The residence must comply with all applicable local ordinances.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.143.14">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14.<span class="headnote">Variances.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may grant a variance to any of the requirements in this section if the conditions in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.04#stat.245A.04.9">245A.04, subdivision 9</a>, are met.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245A.143.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/288/#laws.1.27.0">2004 c 288 art 1 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/56/#laws.0.1.0">2005 c 56 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/147/#laws.7.75.0">2007 c 147 art 7 s 75</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/9/#laws.4.7.0">1Sp2011 c 9 art 4 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/0/115/#laws.4.77.0">2020 c 115 art 4 s 77</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/58/#laws.0.96.0">2022 c 58 s 96</a>-98</p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245A.1434"><b>245A.1434</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.60">142B.60</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245A.1435">
        <h1 class="shn">245A.1435 REDUCTION OF RISK OF SUDDEN UNEXPECTED INFANT DEATH.</h1>
        <p>Programs licensed under this chapter must comply with the requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.46">142B.46</a> whenever caring for infants.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245A.1435.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/112/#laws.0.15.0">2007 c 112 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/142/#laws.2.19.0">2009 c 142 art 2 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.3.14.0">2013 c 108 art 3 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/291/#laws.1.2.0">2014 c 291 art 1 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/0/115/#laws.4.78.0">2020 c 115 art 4 s 78</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/58/#laws.0.99.0">2022 c 58 s 99</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.8.19.0">2023 c 70 art 8 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.2.55.0">2024 c 80 art 2 s 55</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245A.144">
        <h1 class="shn">245A.144 TRAINING ON RISK OF SUDDEN UNEXPECTED INFANT DEATH AND ABUSIVE HEAD TRAUMA FOR CHILD FOSTER CARE PROVIDERS.</h1>
        <p class="in">(a) Licensed child foster care providers that care for infants or children through five years of age must document that before staff persons assist in the care of infants or children through five years of age, they are instructed on the standards in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.46">142B.46 </a>and receive training on reducing the risk of sudden unexpected infant death and abusive head trauma from shaking infants and young children. The training on reducing the risk of sudden unexpected infant death and abusive head trauma may be provided as:</p>
        <p class="in">(1) orientation training to child foster care providers, who care for infants or children through five years of age, under Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.3070">2960.3070</a>, subpart 1; or</p>
        <p class="in">(2) in-service training to child foster care providers, who care for infants or children through five years of age, under Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.3070">2960.3070</a>, subpart 2.</p>
        <p class="in">(b) Training required under this section must be at least one hour in length and must be completed at least once every five years. At a minimum, the training must address the risk factors related to sudden unexpected infant death and abusive head trauma, means of reducing the risk of sudden unexpected infant death and abusive head trauma, and license holder communication with parents regarding reducing the risk of sudden unexpected infant death and abusive head trauma.</p>
        <p class="in">(c) Training for child foster care providers must be approved by the county that is responsible for monitoring the child foster care provider under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.16">245A.16</a>. The approved training fulfills, in part, training required under Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.3070">2960.3070</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245A.144.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/9/#laws.14.23.0">1Sp2001 c 9 art 14 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/375/#laws.1.16.0">2002 c 375 art 1 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/379/#laws.1.113.0">2002 c 379 art 1 s 113</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/4/#laws.1.19.0">1Sp2005 c 4 art 1 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/112/#laws.0.16.0">2007 c 112 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/26/#laws.0.1.0">2009 c 26 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/329/#laws.1.9.0">2010 c 329 art 1 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.3.15.0">2013 c 108 art 3 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.10.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 10</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245A.1443">
        <h1 class="shn">245A.1443 SUBSTANCE USE DISORDER TREATMENT LICENSED PROGRAMS THAT SERVE PARENTS WITH THEIR CHILDREN.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.1443.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Application.</span></h2>
          <p>This section applies to residential substance use disorder treatment facilities that are licensed under this chapter and chapter 245G and that provide services in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245G.19">245G.19</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.1443.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Requirements for providing education.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) On or before the date of a child's initial physical presence at the facility, the license holder must provide education to the child's parent related to safe bathing and reducing the risk of sudden unexpected infant death and abusive head trauma from shaking infants and young children. The license holder must use the educational material developed by the commissioner to comply with this requirement. At a minimum, the education must address:</p>
          <p>(1) instruction that a child or infant should never be left unattended around water, a tub should be filled with only two to four inches of water for infants, and an infant should never be put into a tub when the water is running; and</p>
          <p>(2) the risk factors related to sudden unexpected infant death and abusive head trauma from shaking infants and young children, and means of reducing the risks, including the safety precautions identified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.46">142B.46</a> and the risks of co-sleeping.</p>
          <p>(b) The license holder must document the parent's receipt of the education and keep the documentation in the parent's file. The documentation must indicate whether the parent agrees to comply with the safeguards. If the parent refuses to comply, program staff must provide additional education to the parent as described in the parental supervision plan. The parental supervision plan must include the intervention, frequency, and staff responsible for the duration of the parent's participation in the program or until the parent agrees to comply with the safeguards.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.1443.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Parental supervision of children.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) On or before the date of a child's initial physical presence at the facility, the license holder must document the parent's capacity to meet the health and safety needs of the child while on the facility premises considering the following factors:</p>
          <p>(1) the parent's physical and mental health;</p>
          <p>(2) the parent being under the influence of drugs, alcohol, medications, or other chemicals;</p>
          <p>(3) the child's physical and mental health; and</p>
          <p>(4) any other information available to the license holder that indicates the parent may not be able to adequately care for the child.</p>
          <p>(b) The license holder must have written procedures specifying the actions to be taken by staff if a parent is or becomes unable to adequately care for the parent's child.</p>
          <p>(c) If the parent refuses to comply with the safeguards described in subdivision 2 or is unable to adequately care for the child, the license holder must develop a parental supervision plan in conjunction with the client. The plan must account for any factors in paragraph (a) that contribute to the parent's inability to adequately care for the child. The plan must be dated and signed by the staff person who completed the plan.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.1443.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Alternative supervision arrangements.</span></h2>
          <p>The license holder must have written procedures addressing whether the program permits a parent to arrange for supervision of the parent's child by another client in the program. If permitted, the facility must have a procedure that requires staff approval of the supervision arrangement before the supervision by the nonparental client occurs. The procedure for approval must include an assessment of the nonparental client's capacity to assume the supervisory responsibilities using the criteria in subdivision 3. The license holder must document the license holder's approval of the supervisory arrangement and the assessment of the nonparental client's capacity to supervise the child, and must keep this documentation in the file of the parent of the child being supervised.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245A.1443.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/78/#laws.4.17.0">2015 c 78 art 4 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/182/#laws.2.11.0">2018 c 182 art 2 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/98/#laws.12.3.0">2022 c 98 art 12 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.2.56.0">2024 c 80 art 2 s 56</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245A.1444">
        <h1 class="shn">245A.1444 TRAINING BY OTHER PROGRAMS ON RISK OF SUDDEN UNEXPECTED INFANT DEATH AND ABUSIVE HEAD TRAUMA.</h1>
        <p class="in">A licensed substance use disorder treatment program that serves clients with infants or children through five years of age, who sleep at the program and a licensed children's residential facility that serves infants or children through five years of age, must document that before program staff persons or volunteers assist in the care of infants or children through five years of age, they are instructed on the standards in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.46">142B.46 </a>and receive training on reducing the risk of sudden unexpected infant death and abusive head trauma from shaking infants and young children. The training conducted under this section may be used to fulfill training requirements under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245G.19#stat.245G.19.4">245G.19, subdivision 4</a>, clause (2), and Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0100">2960.0100</a>, subpart 3.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245A.1444.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/112/#laws.0.17.0">2007 c 112 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/26/#laws.0.2.0">2009 c 26 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.3.16.0">2013 c 108 art 3 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/182/#laws.2.12.0">2018 c 182 art 2 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/98/#laws.4.51.0">2022 c 98 art 4 s 51</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.2.57.0">2024 c 80 art 2 s 57</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245A.1445"><b>245A.1445</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.48">142B.48</a>] </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245A.145"><b>245A.145</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.61">142B.61</a>] </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245A.146">
        <h1 class="shn">245A.146 CRIB SAFETY REQUIREMENTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.146.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Crib safety requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner and all licensed children's residential facilities, substance use disorder treatment programs with children in care, and residential habilitation programs serving children with developmental disabilities must follow the requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.45">142B.45</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.146.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.45">142B.45, subd 2</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.146.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.45">142B.45, subd 3</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.146.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.45">142B.45, subd 4</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.146.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.45">142B.45, subd 5</a>] </p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.146.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.45">142B.45, subd 6</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245A.146.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/139/#laws.0.1.0">2005 c 139 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/264/#laws.0.7.0">2006 c 264 s 7</a>,8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.16.9.0">2012 c 216 art 16 s 9</a>,10; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/228/#laws.2.12.0">2014 c 228 art 2 s 12</a>,13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/98/#laws.4.51.0">2022 c 98 art 4 s 51</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.8.20.0">2023 c 70 art 8 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.2.58.0">2024 c 80 art 2 s 58</a>-61</p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245A.147"><b>245A.147</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.75">142B.75</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245A.148"><b>245A.148</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.76">142B.76</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245A.149"><b>245A.149</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.77">142B.77</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245A.15"><b>245A.15</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.78">142B.78</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245A.151">
        <h1 class="shn">245A.151 FIRE MARSHAL INSPECTION.</h1>
        <p>When licensure under this chapter or certification under chapter 142C requires an inspection by a fire marshal to determine compliance with the State Fire Code under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/299F.011">299F.011</a>, a local fire code inspector approved by the state fire marshal may conduct the inspection. If a community does not have a local fire code inspector or if the local fire code inspector does not perform the inspection, the state fire marshal must conduct the inspection. A local fire code inspector or the state fire marshal may recover the cost of these inspections through a fee of no more than $50 per inspection charged to the applicant or license holder or license-exempt child care center certification holder. The fees collected by the state fire marshal under this section are appropriated to the commissioner of public safety for the purpose of conducting the inspections.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245A.151.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/375/#laws.1.17.0">2002 c 375 art 1 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/136/#laws.9.14.0">2005 c 136 art 9 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/9/#laws.2.47.0">1Sp2019 c 9 art 2 s 47</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.3.3.0">2024 c 80 art 3 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245A.1511"><b>245A.1511</b> Renumbered [<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.79">142B.79</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245A.152"><b>245A.152</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.62">142B.62</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245A.153"><b>245A.153</b> MS 2018 [Expired, 1Sp2017 c 6 art 9 s 10]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245A.155">
        <h1 class="shn">245A.155 CARE OF INDIVIDUALS ON MEDICAL MONITORING EQUIPMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.155.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Licensed foster care and respite care.</span></h2>
          <p>This section applies to foster care agencies and licensed foster care providers who place, supervise, or care for individuals who rely on medical monitoring equipment to sustain life or monitor a medical condition that could become life-threatening without proper use of the medical equipment in respite care or foster care.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.155.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Foster care agency requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>In order for an agency to place an individual who relies on medical equipment to sustain life or monitor a medical condition that could become life-threatening without proper use of the medical equipment with a foster care provider, the agency must ensure that the foster care provider has received the training to operate such equipment as observed and confirmed by a qualified source, and that the provider:</p>
          <p>(1) is currently caring for an individual who is using the same equipment in the foster home; or</p>
          <p>(2) has written documentation that the foster care provider has cared for an individual who relied on such equipment within the past six months; or</p>
          <p>(3) has successfully completed training with the individual being placed with the provider.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.155.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Foster care provider requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>A foster care provider shall not care for an individual who relies on medical equipment to sustain life or monitor a medical condition unless the provider has received the training to operate such equipment as observed and confirmed by a qualified source, and:</p>
          <p>(1) is currently caring for an individual who is using the same equipment in the foster home; or</p>
          <p>(2) has written documentation that the foster care provider has cared for an individual who relied on such equipment within the past six months; or</p>
          <p>(3) has successfully completed training with the individual being placed with the provider.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.155.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Qualified source definition.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of this section, a "qualified source" includes a health care professional or an individual who provides training on such equipment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.155.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Foster care provider training and skills form.</span></h2>
          <p>The agency supervising the foster care provider shall keep a training and skills form on file for each foster care provider and update the form annually. The agency placing the individual shall obtain a copy of the training and skills form from the foster care provider or the agency supervising the foster care provider and shall keep it and any updated information on file for the duration of the placement. The form must be made available to the parents or the primary caregiver and social worker of the individual, or the individual, whichever is applicable, in order to make an informed placement decision. The agency shall use the training and skills form developed by the commissioner of human services.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245A.155.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/338/#laws.0.1.0">2000 c 338 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/71/#laws.7.5.0">2015 c 71 art 7 s 5</a>,6</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245A.156">
        <h1 class="shn">245A.156 DISCLOSURE OF COMMUNICABLE DISEASE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.156.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Licensed foster care.</span></h2>
          <p>This section applies to county agencies and individuals who place children or adults who have a known communicable disease, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144.4172#stat.144.4172.2">144.4172, subdivision 2</a>, in foster care settings licensed under this chapter.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.156.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Placing agency's or individual's duties.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144.291">144.291</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144.298">144.298</a>, before a county or individual places a child or adult with a known communicable disease with a licensed foster care provider, the agency or individual must:</p>
          <p>(1) disclose to the foster care license holder the individual's communicable disease; and</p>
          <p>(2) determine that the foster care provider has the ability to provide care to the individual.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245A.156.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/290/#laws.0.1.0">2002 c 290 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/147/#laws.10.15.0">2007 c 147 art 10 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.2.63.0">2024 c 80 art 2 s 63</a>,64</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245A.16">
        <h1 class="shn">245A.16 STANDARDS FOR COUNTY AGENCIES AND PRIVATE AGENCIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.16.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Delegation of authority to agencies.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) County agencies that have been designated by the commissioner to perform licensing functions and activities under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.04">245A.04</a>; to recommend denial of applicants under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.05">245A.05</a>; to issue correction orders, to issue variances, and recommend a conditional license under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.06">245A.06</a>; or to recommend suspending or revoking a license or issuing a fine under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.07">245A.07</a>, shall comply with rules and directives of the commissioner governing those functions and with this section. The following variances are excluded from the delegation of variance authority and may be issued only by the commissioner:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) dual licensure of child foster residence setting and community residential setting;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) until the responsibility for family child foster care transfers to the commissioner of children, youth, and families under Laws 2023, chapter 70, article 12, section 30, dual licensure of family child foster care and family adult foster care;</p>
          <p>(3) until the responsibility for family child care transfers to the commissioner of children, youth, and families under Laws 2023, chapter 70, article 12, section 30, dual licensure of family adult foster care and family child care;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) adult foster care or community residential setting maximum capacity;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) adult foster care or community residential setting minimum age requirement;</p>
          <p class="in">(6) child foster care maximum age requirement;</p>
          <p class="in">(7) variances regarding disqualified individuals;</p>
          <p class="in">(8) the required presence of a caregiver in the adult foster care residence during normal sleeping hours;</p>
          <p class="in">(9) variances to requirements relating to chemical use problems of a license holder or a household member of a license holder; and</p>
          <p>(10) variances to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.46">142B.46</a> for the use of a cradleboard for a cultural accommodation.</p>
          <p>(b) Once the respective responsibilities transfer from the commissioner of human services to the commissioner of children, youth, and families, under Laws 2023, chapter 70, article 12, section 30, the commissioners of human services and children, youth, and families must both approve a variance for dual licensure of family child foster care and family adult foster care or family adult foster care and family child care. Variances under this paragraph are excluded from the delegation of variance authority and may be issued only by both commissioners.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) For family adult day services programs, the commissioner may authorize licensing reviews every two years after a licensee has had at least one annual review.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) A license issued under this section may be issued for up to two years.</p>
          <p>(e) During implementation of chapter 245D, the commissioner shall consider:</p>
          <p>(1) the role of counties in quality assurance;</p>
          <p>(2) the duties of county licensing staff; and</p>
          <p>(3) the possible use of joint powers agreements, according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.59">471.59</a>, with counties through which some licensing duties under chapter 245D may be delegated by the commissioner to the counties.</p>
          <p class="bl">Any consideration related to this paragraph must meet all of the requirements of the corrective action plan ordered by the federal Centers for Medicare and Medicaid Services.</p>
          <p>(f) Licensing authority specific to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245D.06">245D.06, subdivisions 5</a>, 6, 7, and 8, or successor provisions; and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245D.061">245D.061</a> or successor provisions, for family child foster care programs providing out-of-home respite, as identified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245D.03">245D.03</a>, subdivision 1, paragraph (b), clause (1), is excluded from the delegation of authority to county agencies.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.16.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Investigations.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The county or private agency shall conduct timely investigations of allegations of maltreatment of children or adults in programs for which the county or private agency is the commissioner's designated representative and record a disposition of each complaint in accordance with applicable law or rule. The county or private agency shall conduct similar investigations of allegations of violations of rules governing licensure of the program.</p>
          <p>(b) If an investigation conducted under paragraph (a) results in evidence that the commissioner should deny an application or suspend, revoke, or make conditional a license, the county or private agency shall make that recommendation to the commissioner within ten working days.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.16.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Recommendations to commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The county agency shall not make recommendations to the commissioner regarding licensure without first conducting an inspection. The county agency must forward its recommendation to the commissioner regarding the appropriate licensing action within 20 working days of receipt of a completed application.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.16.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Enforcement of commissioner's orders.</span></h2>
          <p>The county or private agency shall enforce the commissioner's orders under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.07">245A.07</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.08#stat.245A.08.5">245A.08, subdivision 5</a>, and chapter 245C, according to the instructions of the commissioner. The county attorney shall assist the county agency in the enforcement and defense of the commissioner's orders under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.07">245A.07</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.08">245A.08</a>, and chapter 245C, according to the instructions of the commissioner, unless a conflict of interest exists between the county attorney and the commissioner. For purposes of this section, a conflict of interest means that the county attorney has a direct or shared financial interest with the license holder or has a personal relationship or family relationship with a party in the licensing action.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.16.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Instruction and technical assistance.</span></h2>
          <p> The commissioner shall provide instruction and technical assistance to county agencies that are subject to this section. County agencies shall cooperate with the commissioner in carrying out this section by ensuring that affected employees participate in instruction and technical assistance provided by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.16.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Certification by commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall ensure that rules are uniformly enforced throughout the state by reviewing each county and private agency for compliance with this section and other applicable laws and rules at least every four years. County agencies that comply with this section shall be certified by the commissioner. If a county agency fails to be certified by the commissioner, the commissioner shall certify a reduction of state administrative aids in an amount up to 20 percent of the county's state portion of Vulnerable Children and Adults Act funding.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.16.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.30">142B.30, subd 7</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.16.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Notice of county recommendation.</span></h2>
          <p>The county or private agency shall provide written notice to the license holder when the agency recommends a licensing action to the commissioner under subdivision 2 or 3. The written notice shall inform the license holder about the process for determining a licensing action and how the license holder will be notified of a licensing action determination. The notice shall include the following:</p>
          <p>(1) that the county or private agency made a recommendation to the commissioner to deny an application or suspend, revoke, or make conditional a license;</p>
          <p>(2) that the commissioner will review the recommendation from the county or private agency and then determine if a licensing action will be issued;</p>
          <p>(3) that the license holder will receive written notice from the commissioner indicating the reasons for the licensing action issued; and</p>
          <p>(4) instructions on how to request reconsideration or appeal, if a licensing action is issued.</p>
          <p class="bl">County or private agency recommendations under this section are classified as confidential data under chapter <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13">13</a> and may only be disclosed as permitted by law.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.16.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.30">142B.30, subd 9</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.16.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Licensing and reporting hub.</span></h2>
          <p>Upon implementation of the provider licensing and reporting hub, county staff who perform licensing functions must use the hub in the manner prescribed by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.16.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.30">142B.30, subd 11</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245A.16.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/333/#laws.0.17.0">1987 c 333 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/282/#laws.2.86.0">1989 c 282 art 2 s 86</a>;       <div class="section" id="stat.245A.167">
        <h1 class="shn">245A.167 PUBLIC FUNDS PROGRAM INTEGRITY MONITORING.</h1>
        <p>(a) An applicant or a license holder that has enrolled to receive public funding reimbursement for services is required to comply with the registration or enrollment requirements as licensing standards.</p>
        <p>(b) Compliance with the licensing standards established under paragraph (a) may be monitored during a licensing investigation or inspection. Noncompliance with these licensure standards may result in:</p>
        <p>(1) a correction order or a conditional license under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.06">245A.06</a>, or sanctions under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.07">245A.07</a>;</p>
        <p>(2) nonpayment of claims submitted by the license holder for public program reimbursement according to the statute applicable to that program;</p>
        <p>(3) recovery of payments made for the service according to the statute applicable to that program;</p>
        <p>(4) disenrollment in the public payment program according to the statute applicable to that program; or</p>
        <p>(5) a referral for other administrative, civil, or criminal penalties as provided by law.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245A.167.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.17.3.0">2012 c 216 art 17 s 3</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245A.17"><b>245A.17</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/513/#laws.9.44.0">1992 c 513 art 9 s 44</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245A.175">
        <h1 class="shn">245A.175 CHILD FOSTER CARE TRAINING REQUIREMENT; MENTAL HEALTH TRAINING; FETAL ALCOHOL SPECTRUM DISORDERS TRAINING.</h1>
        <p class="in">Prior to a nonemergency placement of a child in a foster care home, the child foster care license holder and all staff providing care in foster residence settings must complete two hours of training that addresses the causes, symptoms, and key warning signs of mental health disorders; cultural considerations; and effective approaches for dealing with a child's behaviors. At least one hour of the annual training requirement for the foster residence staff must be on children's mental health issues and treatment. Except for providers and services under chapter <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245D">245D</a>, the annual training must also include at least one hour of training on fetal alcohol spectrum disorders, which must be counted toward the 12 hours of required in-service training per year. Training curriculum shall be approved by the commissioner of human services.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245A.175.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/147/#laws.8.11.0">2007 c 147 art 8 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/78/#laws.4.20.0">2015 c 78 art 4 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/101/#laws.0.1.0">2016 c 101 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/188/#laws.0.1.0">2018 c 188 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.12.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 12</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245A.18">
        <h1 class="shn">245A.18 CHILD PASSENGER RESTRAINT SYSTEMS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.18.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Seat belt and child passenger restraint system use.</span></h2>
          <p>All license holders that transport children must comply with the requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.51#stat.142B.51.1">142B.51, subdivision 1</a>, and license holders that transport a child or children under eight years of age must document training that fulfills the requirements in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.51">142B.51</a>, subdivision 2.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.18.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>MS 2023 Supp [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.2.75.0">2024 c 80 art 2 s 75</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245A.18.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/568/#laws.2.54.0">1990 c 568 art 2 s 54</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/13/#laws.1.31.0">1993 c 13 art 1 s 31</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/4/#laws.1.22.0">1Sp2005 c 4 art 1 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/254/#laws.0.1.0">2006 c 254 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/112/#laws.0.20.0">2007 c 112 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/382/#laws.0.46.0">2010 c 382 s 46</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/385/#laws.0.1.0">2010 c 385 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.16.11.0">2012 c 216 art 16 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/9/#laws.2.49.0">1Sp2019 c 9 art 2 s 49</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.8.25.0">2023 c 70 art 8 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.2.68.0">2024 c 80 art 2 s 68</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245A.19">
        <h1 class="shn">245A.19 HIV TRAINING IN SUBSTANCE USE DISORDER TREATMENT PROGRAM.</h1>
        <p>(a) Applicants and license holders for substance use disorder residential and nonresidential programs must demonstrate compliance with HIV minimum standards before their application is complete. The HIV minimum standards contained in the HIV-1 Guidelines for substance use disorder treatment and care programs in Minnesota are not subject to rulemaking.</p>
        <p>(b) The applicant or license holder shall orient all substance use disorder treatment staff and clients to the HIV minimum standards. Thereafter, orientation shall be provided to all staff and clients, within 72 hours of employment or admission to the program. In-service training shall be provided to all staff on at least an annual basis and the license holder shall maintain records of training and attendance.</p>
        <p>(c) The license holder shall maintain a list of referral sources for the purpose of making necessary referrals of clients to HIV-related services. The list of referral services shall be updated at least annually.</p>
        <p>(d) Written policies and procedures, consistent with HIV minimum standards, shall be developed and followed by the license holder. All policies and procedures concerning HIV minimum standards shall be approved by the commissioner. The commissioner must outline the content required in the annual staff training under paragraph (b).</p>
        <p>(e) The commissioner may permit variances from the requirements in this section. License holders seeking variances must follow the procedures in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.04#stat.245A.04.9">245A.04, subdivision 9</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245A.19.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/513/#laws.9.17.0">1992 c 513 art 9 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/98/#laws.4.51.0">2022 c 98 art 4 s 51</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/49/#laws.0.3.0">2023 c 49 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245A.191">
        <h1 class="shn">245A.191 PROVIDER ELIGIBILITY FOR PAYMENTS FROM THE BEHAVIORAL HEALTH FUND.</h1>
        <p>(a) When a substance use disorder treatment provider licensed under this chapter, and governed by the standards of chapter 245G or Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0430">2960.0430</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0490">2960.0490</a>, agrees to meet the applicable requirements under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/254B.05#stat.254B.05.5">254B.05, subdivision 5</a>, to be eligible for enhanced funding from the behavioral health fund, the applicable requirements under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/254B.05">254B.05</a> are also licensing requirements that may be monitored for compliance through licensing investigations and licensing inspections.</p>
        <p class="in">(b) Noncompliance with the requirements identified under paragraph (a) may result in:</p>
        <p class="in">(1) a correction order or a conditional license under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.06">245A.06</a>, or sanctions under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.07">245A.07</a>;</p>
        <p class="in">(2) nonpayment of claims submitted by the license holder for public program reimbursement;</p>
        <p class="in">(3) recovery of payments made for the service;</p>
        <p class="in">(4) disenrollment in the public payment program; or</p>
        <p class="in">(5) other administrative, civil, or criminal penalties as provided by law.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245A.191.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.16.12.0">2012 c 216 art 16 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/21/#laws.1.45.0">2015 c 21 art 1 s 45</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/6/#laws.8.13.0">1Sp2017 c 6 art 8 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.13.83.0">2021 c 30 art 13 s 83</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245A.1915"><b>245A.1915</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/6/#laws.8.77.0">1Sp2017 c 6 art 8 s 77</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245A.192"><b>245A.192</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/6/#laws.8.77.0">1Sp2017 c 6 art 8 s 77</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245A.20"><b>245A.20</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/248/#laws.0.51.0">1997 c 248 s 51</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245A.21"><b>245A.21</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/248/#laws.0.51.0">1997 c 248 s 51</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245A.211">
        <h1 class="shn">245A.211 PRONE RESTRAINT PROHIBITION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.211.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Applicability.</span></h2>
          <p>This section applies to all programs licensed or certified under this chapter, chapters 142C, 245D, 245F, 245G, and sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245I.20">245I.20</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245I.23">245I.23</a>. The requirements in this section are in addition to any applicable requirements for the use of holds or restraints for each license or certification type.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.211.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) "Mechanical restraint" means a restraint device that limits the voluntary movement of a person or the person's limbs.</p>
          <p>(b) "Prone restraint" means a restraint that places a person in a face-down position with the person's chest in contact with the floor or other surface.</p>
          <p>(c) "Restraint" means a physical hold, physical restraint, manual restraint, restraint equipment, or mechanical restraint that holds a person immobile or limits the voluntary movement of a person or the person's limbs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.211.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Prone restraint prohibition.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A license or certification holder must not use a prone restraint on any person receiving services in a program, except in the instances allowed by paragraphs (b) to (d).</p>
          <p>(b) If a person rolls into a prone position during the use of a restraint, the person must be restored to a nonprone position as quickly as possible.</p>
          <p>(c) If the applicable licensing requirements allow a program to use mechanical restraints, a person may be briefly held in a prone restraint for the purpose of applying mechanical restraints if the person is restored to a nonprone position as quickly as possible.</p>
          <p>(d) If the applicable licensing requirements allow a program to use seclusion, a person may be briefly held in a prone restraint to allow staff to safely exit a seclusion room.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.211.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Contraindicated physical restraints.</span></h2>
          <p>A license or certification holder must not implement a restraint on a person receiving services in a program in a way that is contraindicated for any of the person's known medical or psychological conditions. Prior to using restraints on a person with a known medical or psychological conditions that restraints are contraindicated for, the license or certification holder must document the contraindication and the type of restraints that will not be used on the person based on this determination.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245A.211.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.17.19.0">2023 c 70 art 17 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.3.3.0">2024 c 80 art 3 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.62.12.0">2024 c 127 art 62 s 12</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245A.22"><b>245A.22</b> MS 2022 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.17.63.0">2023 c 70 art 17 s 63</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245A.23"><b>245A.23</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.63">142B.63</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245A.24">
        <h1 class="shn">245A.24 MANDATORY REPORTING.</h1>
        <p>Any individual engaging in licensing functions and activities under this chapter, including authorities delegated under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.16">245A.16</a>, must immediately report any suspected fraud to county human services investigators or the Department of Human Services Office of Inspector General.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245A.24.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/9/#laws.2.50.0">1Sp2019 c 9 art 2 s 50</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245A.242">
        <h1 class="shn">245A.242 EMERGENCY OVERDOSE TREATMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.242.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Applicability.</span></h2>
          <p>This section applies to the following licenses issued under this chapter:</p>
          <p>(1) substance use disorder treatment programs licensed according to chapter 245G;</p>
          <p>(2) children's residential facility substance use disorder treatment programs licensed according to Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0010">2960.0010</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0220">2960.0220</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0430">2960.0430</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0490">2960.0490</a>;</p>
          <p>(3) detoxification programs licensed according to Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9530.6510">9530.6510</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9530.6590">9530.6590</a>;</p>
          <p>(4) withdrawal management programs licensed according to chapter 245F; and</p>
          <p>(5) intensive residential treatment services or residential crisis stabilization licensed according to chapter 245I and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245I.23">245I.23</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.242.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Emergency overdose treatment.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A license holder must maintain a supply of opiate antagonists as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/604A.04#stat.604A.04.1">604A.04, subdivision 1</a>, available for emergency treatment of opioid overdose and must have a written standing order protocol by a physician who is licensed under chapter 147, advanced practice registered nurse who is licensed under chapter 148, or physician assistant who is licensed under chapter 147A, that permits the license holder to maintain a supply of opiate antagonists on site. A license holder must require staff to undergo training in the specific mode of administration used at the program, which may include intranasal administration, intramuscular injection, or both.</p>
          <p>(b) Notwithstanding any requirements to the contrary in Minnesota Rules, chapters 2960 and 9530, and Minnesota Statutes, chapters 245F, 245G, and 245I:</p>
          <p>(1) emergency opiate antagonist medications are not required to be stored in a locked area and staff and adult clients may carry this medication on them and store it in an unlocked location;</p>
          <p>(2) staff persons who only administer emergency opiate antagonist medications only require the training required by paragraph (a), which any knowledgeable trainer may provide. The trainer is not required to be a registered nurse or part of an accredited educational institution; and</p>
          <p>(3) nonresidential substance use disorder treatment programs that do not administer client medications beyond emergency opiate antagonist medications are not required to have the policies and procedures required in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245G.08">245G.08, subdivisions 5</a> and 6, and must instead describe the program's procedures for administering opiate antagonist medications in the license holder's description of health care services under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245G.08">245G.08</a>, subdivision 1.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245A.242.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/61/#laws.5.6.0">2023 c 61 art 5 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.62.13.0">2024 c 127 art 62 s 13</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245A.245">
        <h1 class="shn">245A.245 CHILDREN'S RESIDENTIAL FACILITY SUBSTANCE USE DISORDER TREATMENT PROGRAMS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.245.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Applicability.</span></h2>
          <p>A license holder of a children's residential facility substance use disorder treatment program license issued under this chapter and Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0010">2960.0010</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0220">2960.0220</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0430">2960.0430</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0490">2960.0490</a>, must comply with this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.245.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Former students.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) "Alcohol and drug counselor" means an individual qualified according to Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0460">2960.0460</a>, subpart 5.</p>
          <p>(b) "Former student" means an individual that meets the requirements in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148F.11">148F.11</a>, subdivision 2a, to practice as a former student.</p>
          <p>(c) An alcohol and drug counselor must supervise and be responsible for a treatment service performed by a former student and must review and sign each assessment, individual treatment plan, progress note, and treatment plan review prepared by a former student.</p>
          <p>(d) A former student must receive the orientation and training required for permanent staff members.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245A.245.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.6.33.0">2023 c 70 art 6 s 33</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245A.25">
        <h1 class="shn">245A.25 RESIDENTIAL PROGRAM CERTIFICATIONS FOR COMPLIANCE WITH THE FAMILY FIRST PREVENTION SERVICES ACT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.25.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Certification scope and applicability.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) This section establishes the requirements that a children's residential facility or child foster residence setting must meet to be certified for the purposes of Title IV-E funding requirements as:</p>
          <p>(1) a qualified residential treatment program;</p>
          <p>(2) a residential setting specializing in providing care and supportive services for youth who have been or are at risk of becoming victims of sex trafficking or commercial sexual exploitation;</p>
          <p>(3) a residential setting specializing in providing prenatal, postpartum, or parenting support for youth; or</p>
          <p>(4) a supervised independent living setting for youth who are 18 years of age or older.</p>
          <p>(b) This section does not apply to a foster family setting in which the license holder resides in the foster home.</p>
          <p>(c) Children's residential facilities licensed as detention settings according to Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0230">2960.0230</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0290">2960.0290</a>, or secure programs according to Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0300">2960.0300</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0420">2960.0420</a>, may not be certified under this section.</p>
          <p>(d) For purposes of this section, "license holder" means an individual, organization, or government entity that was issued a children's residential facility or foster residence setting license by the commissioner of human services under this chapter or by the commissioner of corrections under chapter 241.</p>
          <p>(e) Certifications issued under this section for foster residence settings may only be issued by the commissioner of human services and are not delegated to county or private licensing agencies under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.16">245A.16</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.25.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Program certification types and requests for certification.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) By July 1, 2021, the commissioner of human services must offer certifications to license holders for the following types of programs:</p>
          <p>(1) qualified residential treatment programs;</p>
          <p>(2) residential settings specializing in providing care and supportive services for youth who have been or are at risk of becoming victims of sex trafficking or commercial sexual exploitation;</p>
          <p>(3) residential settings specializing in providing prenatal, postpartum, or parenting support for youth; and</p>
          <p>(4) supervised independent living settings for youth who are 18 years of age or older.</p>
          <p>(b) An applicant or license holder must submit a request for certification under this section on a form and in a manner prescribed by the commissioner of human services. The decision of the commissioner of human services to grant or deny a certification request is final and not subject to appeal under chapter 14.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.25.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Trauma-informed care.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Programs certified under subdivision 4 or 5 must provide services to a person according to a trauma-informed model of care that meets the requirements of this subdivision, except that programs certified under subdivision 5 are not required to meet the requirements of paragraph (e).</p>
          <p>(b) For the purposes of this section, "trauma-informed care" means care that:</p>
          <p>(1) acknowledges the effects of trauma on a person receiving services and on the person's family;</p>
          <p>(2) modifies services to respond to the effects of trauma on the person receiving services;</p>
          <p>(3) emphasizes skill and strength-building rather than symptom management; and</p>
          <p>(4) focuses on the physical and psychological safety of the person receiving services and the person's family.</p>
          <p>(c) The license holder must have a process for identifying the signs and symptoms of trauma in a youth and must address the youth's needs related to trauma. This process must include:</p>
          <p>(1) screening for trauma by completing a trauma-specific screening tool with each youth upon the youth's admission or obtaining the results of a trauma-specific screening tool that was completed with the youth within 30 days prior to the youth's admission to the program; and</p>
