          <p>(2) ensuring that trauma-based interventions targeting specific trauma-related symptoms are available to each youth when needed to assist the youth in obtaining services. For qualified residential treatment programs, this must include the provision of services in paragraph (e).</p>
          <p>(d) The license holder must develop and provide services to each youth according to the principles of trauma-informed care including:</p>
          <p>(1) recognizing the impact of trauma on a youth when determining the youth's service needs and providing services to the youth;</p>
          <p>(2) allowing each youth to participate in reviewing and developing the youth's individualized treatment or service plan;</p>
          <p>(3) providing services to each youth that are person-centered and culturally responsive; and</p>
          <p>(4) adjusting services for each youth to address additional needs of the youth.</p>
          <p>(e) In addition to the other requirements of this subdivision, qualified residential treatment programs must use a trauma-based treatment model that includes:</p>
          <p>(1) assessing each youth to determine if the youth needs trauma-specific treatment interventions;</p>
          <p>(2) identifying in each youth's treatment plan how the program will provide trauma-specific treatment interventions to the youth;</p>
          <p>(3) providing trauma-specific treatment interventions to a youth that target the youth's specific trauma-related symptoms; and</p>
          <p>(4) training all clinical staff of the program on trauma-specific treatment interventions.</p>
          <p>(f) At the license holder's program, the license holder must provide a physical, social, and emotional environment that:</p>
          <p>(1) promotes the physical and psychological safety of each youth;</p>
          <p>(2) avoids aspects that may be retraumatizing;</p>
          <p>(3) responds to trauma experienced by each youth and the youth's other needs; and</p>
          <p>(4) includes designated spaces that are available to each youth for engaging in sensory and self-soothing activities.</p>
          <p>(g) The license holder must base the program's policies and procedures on trauma-informed principles. In the program's policies and procedures, the license holder must:</p>
          <p>(1) describe how the program provides services according to a trauma-informed model of care;</p>
          <p>(2) describe how the program's environment fulfills the requirements of paragraph (f);</p>
          <p>(3) prohibit the use of aversive consequences for a youth's violation of program rules or any other reason;</p>
          <p>(4) describe the process for how the license holder incorporates trauma-informed principles and practices into the organizational culture of the license holder's program; and</p>
          <p>(5) if the program is certified to use restrictive procedures under Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0710">2960.0710</a>, describe how the program uses restrictive procedures only when necessary for a youth in a manner that addresses the youth's history of trauma and avoids causing the youth additional trauma.</p>
          <p>(h) Prior to allowing a staff person to have direct contact, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245C.02">245C.02</a>, subdivision 11, with a youth and annually thereafter, the license holder must train each staff person about:</p>
          <p>(1) concepts of trauma-informed care and how to provide services to each youth according to these concepts; and</p>
          <p>(2) impacts of each youth's culture, race, gender, and sexual orientation on the youth's behavioral health and traumatic experiences.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.25.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Qualified residential treatment programs; certification requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) To be certified as a qualified residential treatment program, a license holder must meet:</p>
          <p>(1) the definition of a qualified residential treatment program in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.007">260C.007</a>, subdivision 26d;</p>
          <p>(2) the requirements for providing trauma-informed care and using a trauma-based treatment model in subdivision 3; and</p>
          <p>(3) the requirements of this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(b) For each youth placed in the license holder's program, the license holder must collaborate with the responsible social services agency and other appropriate parties to implement the youth's out-of-home placement plan and the youth's short-term and long-term mental health and behavioral health goals in the assessment required by sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212">260C.212</a>, subdivision 1; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.704">260C.704</a>; and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.708">260C.708</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) A qualified residential treatment program must use a trauma-based treatment model that meets all of the requirements of subdivision 3 that is designed to address the needs, including clinical needs, of youth with serious emotional or behavioral disorders or disturbances. The license holder must develop, document, and review a treatment plan for each youth according to the requirements of Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0180">2960.0180</a>, subpart 2, item B; and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0190">2960.0190</a>, subpart 2.</p>
          <p>(d) The following types of staff must be on-site according to the program's treatment model and must be available 24 hours a day and seven days a week to provide care within the scope of their practice:</p>
          <p>(1) a registered nurse or licensed practical nurse licensed by the Minnesota Board of Nursing to practice professional nursing or practical nursing as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/148.171">148.171</a>, subdivisions 14 and 15; and</p>
          <p>(2) other licensed clinical staff to meet each youth's clinical needs.</p>
          <p>(e) A qualified residential treatment program must be accredited by one of the following independent, not-for-profit organizations:</p>
          <p>(1) the Commission on Accreditation of Rehabilitation Facilities (CARF);</p>
          <p>(2) the Joint Commission;</p>
          <p>(3) the Council on Accreditation (COA); or</p>
          <p>(4) another independent, not-for-profit accrediting organization approved by the Secretary of the United States Department of Health and Human Services.</p>
          <p>(f) The license holder must facilitate participation of a youth's family members in the youth's treatment program, consistent with the youth's best interests and according to the youth's out-of-home placement plan required by sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212#stat.260C.212.1">260C.212, subdivision 1</a>; and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.708">260C.708</a>.</p>
          <p>(g) The license holder must contact and facilitate outreach to each youth's family members, including the youth's siblings, and must document outreach to the youth's family members in the youth's file, including the contact method and each family member's contact information. In the youth's file, the license holder must record and maintain the contact information for all known biological family members and fictive kin of the youth.</p>
          <p>(h) The license holder must document in the youth's file how the program integrates family members into the treatment process for the youth, including after the youth's discharge from the program, and how the program maintains the youth's connections to the youth's siblings.</p>
          <p>(i) The program must provide discharge planning and family-based aftercare support to each youth for at least six months after the youth's discharge from the program. When providing aftercare to a youth, the program must have monthly contact with the youth and the youth's caregivers to promote the youth's engagement in aftercare services and to regularly evaluate the family's needs. The program's monthly contact with the youth may be face-to-face, by telephone, or virtual.</p>
          <p>(j) The license holder must maintain a service delivery plan that describes how the program provides services according to the requirements in paragraphs (b) to (i).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.25.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Residential settings specializing in providing care and supportive services for youth who have been or are at risk of becoming victims of sex trafficking or commercial sexual exploitation; certification requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) To be certified as a residential setting specializing in providing care and supportive services for youth who have been or are at risk of becoming victims of sex trafficking or commercial sexual exploitation, a license holder must meet the requirements of this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(b) Settings certified according to this subdivision are exempt from the requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.04#stat.245A.04.11">245A.04, subdivision 11</a>, paragraph (b).</p>
          <p>(c) The program must use a trauma-informed model of care that meets all of the applicable requirements of subdivision 3, and that is designed to address the needs, including emotional and mental health needs, of youth who have been or are at risk of becoming victims of sex trafficking or commercial sexual exploitation.</p>
          <p>(d) The program must provide high-quality care and supportive services for youth who have been or are at risk of becoming victims of sex trafficking or commercial sexual exploitation and must:</p>
          <p>(1) offer a safe setting to each youth designed to prevent ongoing and future trafficking of the youth;</p>
          <p>(2) provide equitable, culturally responsive, and individualized services to each youth;</p>
          <p>(3) assist each youth with accessing medical, mental health, legal, advocacy, and family services based on the youth's individual needs;</p>
          <p>(4) provide each youth with relevant educational, life skills, and employment supports based on the youth's individual needs;</p>
          <p>(5) offer a trafficking prevention education curriculum and provide support for each youth at risk of future sex trafficking or commercial sexual exploitation; and</p>
          <p>(6) engage with the discharge planning process for each youth and the youth's family.</p>
          <p>(e) The license holder must maintain a service delivery plan that describes how the program provides services according to the requirements in paragraphs (c) and (d).</p>
          <p>(f) The license holder must ensure that each staff person who has direct contact, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245C.02#stat.245C.02.11">245C.02, subdivision 11</a>, with a youth served by the license holder's program completes a human trafficking training approved by the Department of Human Services' Children and Family Services Administration before the staff person has direct contact with a youth served by the program and annually thereafter. For programs certified prior to January 1, 2022, the license holder must ensure that each staff person at the license holder's program completes the initial training by January 1, 2022.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.25.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Residential settings specializing in providing prenatal, postpartum, or parenting supports for youth; certification requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) To be certified as a residential setting specializing in providing prenatal, postpartum, or parenting supports for youth, a license holder must meet the requirements of this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(b) The license holder must collaborate with the responsible social services agency and other appropriate parties to implement each youth's out-of-home placement plan required by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.212#stat.260C.212.1">260C.212, subdivision 1</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) The license holder must specialize in providing prenatal, postpartum, or parenting supports for youth and must:</p>
          <p>(1) provide equitable, culturally responsive, and individualized services to each youth;</p>
          <p>(2) assist each youth with accessing postpartum services during the same period of time that a woman is considered pregnant for the purposes of medical assistance eligibility under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.055#stat.256B.055.6">256B.055, subdivision 6</a>, including providing each youth with:</p>
          <p>(i) sexual and reproductive health services and education; and</p>
          <p>(ii) a postpartum mental health assessment and follow-up services; and</p>
          <p>(3) discharge planning that includes the youth and the youth's family.</p>
          <p>(d) On or before the date of a child's initial physical presence at the facility, the license holder must provide education to the child's parent related to safe bathing and reducing the risk of sudden unexpected infant death and abusive head trauma from shaking infants and young children. The license holder must use the educational material developed by the commissioner of human services to comply with this requirement. At a minimum, the education must address:</p>
          <p>(1) instruction that: (i) a child or infant should never be left unattended around water; (ii) a tub should be filled with only two to four inches of water for infants; and (iii) an infant should never be put into a tub when the water is running; and</p>
          <p>(2) the risk factors related to sudden unexpected infant death and abusive head trauma from shaking infants and young children and means of reducing the risks, including the safety precautions identified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.46">142B.46</a> and the risks of co-sleeping.</p>
          <p class="bl">The license holder must document the parent's receipt of the education and keep the documentation in the parent's file. The documentation must indicate whether the parent agrees to comply with the safeguards described in this paragraph. If the parent refuses to comply, program staff must provide additional education to the parent as described in the parental supervision plan. The parental supervision plan must include the intervention, frequency, and staff responsible for the duration of the parent's participation in the program or until the parent agrees to comply with the safeguards described in this paragraph.</p>
          <p>(e) On or before the date of a child's initial physical presence at the facility, the license holder must document the parent's capacity to meet the health and safety needs of the child while on the facility premises considering the following factors:</p>
          <p>(1) the parent's physical and mental health;</p>
          <p>(2) the parent being under the influence of drugs, alcohol, medications, or other chemicals;</p>
          <p>(3) the child's physical and mental health; and</p>
          <p>(4) any other information available to the license holder indicating that the parent may not be able to adequately care for the child.</p>
          <p>(f) The license holder must have written procedures specifying the actions that staff shall take if a parent is or becomes unable to adequately care for the parent's child.</p>
          <p>(g) If the parent refuses to comply with the safeguards described in paragraph (d) or is unable to adequately care for the child, the license holder must develop a parental supervision plan in conjunction with the parent. The plan must account for any factors in paragraph (e) that contribute to the parent's inability to adequately care for the child. The plan must be dated and signed by the staff person who completed the plan.</p>
          <p>(h) The license holder must have written procedures addressing whether the program permits a parent to arrange for supervision of the parent's child by another youth in the program. If permitted, the facility must have a procedure that requires staff approval of the supervision arrangement before the supervision by the nonparental youth occurs. The procedure for approval must include an assessment of the nonparental youth's capacity to assume the supervisory responsibilities using the criteria in paragraph (e). The license holder must document the license holder's approval of the supervisory arrangement and the assessment of the nonparental youth's capacity to supervise the child and must keep this documentation in the file of the parent whose child is being supervised by the nonparental youth.</p>
          <p>(i) The license holder must maintain a service delivery plan that describes how the program provides services according to paragraphs (b) to (h).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.25.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Supervised independent living settings for youth 18 years of age or older; certification requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) To be certified as a supervised independent living setting for youth who are 18 years of age or older, a license holder must meet the requirements of this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(b) A license holder must provide training, counseling, instruction, supervision, and assistance for independent living, according to the needs of the youth being served.</p>
          <p>(c) A license holder may provide services to assist the youth with locating housing, money management, meal preparation, shopping, health care, transportation, and any other support services necessary to meet the youth's needs and improve the youth's ability to conduct such tasks independently.</p>
          <p>(d) The service plan for the youth must contain an objective of independent living skills.</p>
          <p>(e) The license holder must maintain a service delivery plan that describes how the program provides services according to paragraphs (b) to (d).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.25.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Monitoring and inspections.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For a program licensed by the commissioner of human services, the commissioner of human services may review a program's compliance with certification requirements by conducting an inspection, a licensing review, or an investigation of the program. The commissioner may issue a correction order to the license holder for a program's noncompliance with the certification requirements of this section. For a program licensed by the commissioner of human services, a license holder must make a request for reconsideration of a correction order according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.06">245A.06, subdivision </a>2.</p>
          <p>(b) For a program licensed by the commissioner of children, youth, and families or the commissioner of corrections, the commissioner of human services may review the program's compliance with the requirements for a certification issued under this section biennially and may issue a correction order identifying the program's noncompliance with the requirements of this section. The correction order must state the following:</p>
          <p>(1) the conditions that constitute a violation of a law or rule;</p>
          <p>(2) the specific law or rule violated; and</p>
          <p>(3) the time allowed for the program to correct each violation.</p>
          <p>(c) For a program licensed by the commissioner of children, youth, and families or the commissioner of corrections, if a license holder believes that there are errors in the correction order of the commissioner of human services, the license holder may ask the Department of Human Services to reconsider the parts of the correction order that the license holder alleges are in error. To submit a request for reconsideration, the license holder must send a written request for reconsideration by United States mail to the commissioner of human services. The request for reconsideration must be postmarked within 20 calendar days of the date that the correction order was received by the license holder and must:</p>
          <p>(1) specify the parts of the correction order that are alleged to be in error;</p>
          <p>(2) explain why the parts of the correction order are in error; and</p>
          <p>(3) include documentation to support the allegation of error.</p>
          <p class="bl">A request for reconsideration does not stay any provisions or requirements of the correction order. The commissioner of human services' disposition of a request for reconsideration is final and not subject to appeal under chapter 14.</p>
          <p>(d) Nothing in this subdivision prohibits the commissioner of human services from decertifying a license holder according to subdivision 9 prior to issuing a correction order.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.25.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Decertification.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner of human services may rescind a certification issued under this section if a license holder fails to comply with the certification requirements in this section.</p>
          <p>(b) The license holder may request reconsideration of a decertification by notifying the commissioner of human services by certified mail or personal service. The license holder must request reconsideration of a decertification in writing. If the license holder sends the request for reconsideration of a decertification by certified mail, the license holder must send the request by United States mail to the commissioner of human services and the request must be postmarked within 20 calendar days after the license holder received the notice of decertification. If the license holder requests reconsideration of a decertification by personal service, the request for reconsideration must be received by the commissioner of human services within 20 calendar days after the license holder received the notice of decertification. When submitting a request for reconsideration of a decertification, the license holder must submit a written argument or evidence in support of the request for reconsideration.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner of human services' disposition of a request for reconsideration is final and not subject to appeal under chapter 14.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.25.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Variances.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of human services may grant variances to the requirements in this section that do not affect a youth's health or safety or compliance with federal requirements for Title IV-E funding if the conditions in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.04">245A.04, subdivision </a>9, are met.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245A.25.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.10.10.0">2021 c 30 art 10 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.2.69.0">2024 c 80 art 2 s 69</a>-71; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.47.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 47</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245A.26">
        <h1 class="shn">245A.26 CHILDREN'S RESIDENTIAL FACILITY CRISIS STABILIZATION SERVICES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.26.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For the purposes of this section, the terms defined in this subdivision have the meanings given.</p>
          <p>(b) "Clinical trainee" means a staff person who is qualified under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245I.04">245I.04</a>, subdivision 6.</p>
          <p>(c) "License holder" means an individual, organization, or government entity that was issued a license by the commissioner of human services under this chapter for residential mental health treatment for children with emotional disturbance according to Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0010">2960.0010</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0220">2960.0220</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0580">2960.0580</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0700">2960.0700</a>, or shelter care services according to Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0010">2960.0010</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0120">2960.0120</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0510">2960.0510</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0530">2960.0530</a>.</p>
          <p>(d) "Mental health professional" means an individual who is qualified under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245I.04#stat.245I.04.2">245I.04, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.26.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Scope and applicability.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) This section establishes additional licensing requirements for a children's residential facility to provide children's residential crisis stabilization services to a client who is experiencing a mental health crisis and is in need of residential treatment services.</p>
          <p>(b) A children's residential facility may provide residential crisis stabilization services only if the facility is licensed to provide:</p>
          <p>(1) residential mental health treatment for children with emotional disturbance according to Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0010">2960.0010</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0220">2960.0220</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0580">2960.0580</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0700">2960.0700</a>; or</p>
          <p>(2) shelter care services according to Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0010">2960.0010</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0120">2960.0120 </a>and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0510">2960.0510</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0530">2960.0530</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) If a client receives residential crisis stabilization services for 35 days or fewer in a facility licensed according to paragraph (b), clause (1), the facility is not required to complete a diagnostic assessment or treatment plan under Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0180">2960.0180</a>, subpart 2, and part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0600">2960.0600</a>.</p>
          <p>(d) If a client receives residential crisis stabilization services for 35 days or fewer in a facility licensed according to paragraph (b), clause (2), the facility is not required to develop a plan for meeting the client's immediate needs under Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0520">2960.0520</a>, subpart 3.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.26.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Eligibility for services.</span></h2>
          <p>An individual is eligible for children's residential crisis stabilization services if the individual is under 21 years of age and meets the eligibility criteria for crisis services under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0624#stat.256B.0624.3">256B.0624, subdivision 3</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.26.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Required services; providers.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A license holder providing residential crisis stabilization services must continually follow a client's individual crisis treatment plan to improve the client's functioning.</p>
          <p>(b) The license holder must offer and have the capacity to directly provide the following treatment services to a client:</p>
          <p>(1) crisis stabilization services as described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0624#stat.256B.0624.7">256B.0624, subdivision 7</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) mental health services as specified in the client's individual crisis treatment plan, according to the client's treatment needs;</p>
          <p>(3) health services and medication administration, if applicable; and</p>
          <p>(4) referrals for the client to community-based treatment providers and support services for the client's transition from residential crisis stabilization to another treatment setting.</p>
          <p>(c) Children's residential crisis stabilization services must be provided by a qualified staff person listed in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0624#stat.256B.0624.8">256B.0624, subdivision 8</a>, according to the scope of practice for the individual staff person's position.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.26.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Assessment and treatment planning.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Within 12 hours of a client's admission for residential crisis stabilization, the license holder must assess the client and document the client's immediate needs, including the client's:</p>
          <p>(1) health and safety, including the need for crisis assistance;</p>
          <p>(2) need for connection to family and other natural supports;</p>
          <p>(3) if applicable, housing and legal issues; and</p>
          <p>(4) if applicable, responsibilities for children, family, and other natural supports, and employers.</p>
          <p>(b) Within 24 hours of a client's admission for residential crisis stabilization, the license holder must complete a crisis treatment plan for the client, according to the requirements for a crisis treatment plan under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0624#stat.256B.0624.11">256B.0624, subdivision 11</a>. The license holder must base the client's crisis treatment plan on the client's referral information and the assessment of the client's immediate needs under paragraph (a). A mental health professional or a clinical trainee under the supervision of a mental health professional must complete the crisis treatment plan. A crisis treatment plan completed by a clinical trainee must contain documentation of approval, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245I.02#stat.245I.02.2">245I.02, subdivision 2</a>, by a mental health professional within five business days of initial completion by the clinical trainee.</p>
          <p>(c) A mental health professional must review a client's crisis treatment plan each week and document the weekly reviews in the client's client file.</p>
          <p>(d) For a client receiving children's residential crisis stabilization services who is 18 years of age or older, the license holder must complete an individual abuse prevention plan for the client, pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.65#stat.245A.65.2">245A.65, subdivision 2</a>, as part of the client's crisis treatment plan.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.26.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Staffing requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>Staff members of facilities providing services under this section must have access to a mental health professional or clinical trainee within 30 minutes, either in person or by telephone. The license holder must maintain a current schedule of available mental health professionals or clinical trainees and include contact information for each mental health professional or clinical trainee. The schedule must be readily available to all staff members.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245A.26.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/99/#laws.1.12.0">2022 c 99 art 1 s 12</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">JUVENILE FACILITIES LICENSING PROHIBITION</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245A.30">
        <h1 class="shn">245A.30 LICENSING PROHIBITION FOR CERTAIN FACILITIES SERVING CHILDREN.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner may not:</p>
        <p>(1) issue any license under Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0010">2960.0010</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0710">2960.0710</a>, for the residential placement of children at a facility if the facility accepts children who reside outside of Minnesota without an agreement with the entity placing the child at the facility that obligates the entity to pay the educational and medical expenses of the child; or</p>
        <p>(2) renew a license under Minnesota Rules, parts <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0010">2960.0010</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.0710">2960.0710</a>, for the residential placement of children if the facility accepts children who reside outside of Minnesota without an agreement with the entity placing the child at the facility that obligates the entity to pay the educational and medical expenses of the child.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245A.30.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/367/#laws.10.3.0">1998 c 367 art 10 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/245/#laws.8.2.0">1999 c 245 art 8 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/329/#laws.1.10.0">2010 c 329 art 1 s 10</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr_by_subd" id="stat.245A.40">
        <h1>245A.40</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.40.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.65#stat.142B.65.1">142B.65, subdivision 1</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.40.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.65">142B.65, subd 2</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.40.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.65">142B.65, subd 3</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.40.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.65">142B.65, subd 4</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.40.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.65"> 142B.65, subd 5</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.40.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.65">142B.65, subd 6</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.40.5a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5a.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.65">142B.65, subd 7</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.40.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.65">142B.65, subd 8</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.40.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.65">142B.65, subd 9</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.40.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.</h2>
          <p>MS 2018 [Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/9/#laws.2.51.0">1Sp2019 c 9 art 2 s 51</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.40.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.</h2>
          <p>MS 2018 [Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/9/#laws.2.51.0">1Sp2019 c 9 art 2 s 51</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.40.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.65">142B.65, subd 10</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245A.41"><b>245A.41</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.66">142B.66</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245A.42"><b>245A.42</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.67">142B.67</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245A.50"><b>245A.50</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.70">142B.70</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245A.51"><b>245A.51</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.71">142B.71</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245A.52"><b>245A.52</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.72">142B.72</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245A.53"><b>245A.53</b> [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.74">142B.74</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">VULNERABLE ADULTS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245A.65">
        <h1 class="shn">245A.65 MALTREATMENT OF VULNERABLE ADULTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.65.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">License holder requirements.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">All license holders serving vulnerable adults shall establish and enforce written policies and procedures related to suspected or alleged maltreatment, and shall orient clients and mandated reporters who are under the control of the license holder to these procedures, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/626.5572#stat.626.5572.16">626.5572, subdivision 16</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(a) License holders must establish policies and procedures allowing but not mandating the internal reporting of alleged or suspected maltreatment. License holders shall ensure that the policies and procedures on internal reporting:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) meet all the requirements identified for the optional internal reporting policies and procedures in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/626.557#stat.626.557.4a">626.557, subdivision 4a</a>; and</p>
          <p class="in">(2) identify the primary and secondary person or position to whom internal reports may be made and the primary and secondary person or position responsible for forwarding internal reports to the common entry point as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/626.5572#stat.626.5572.5">626.5572, subdivision 5</a>. The secondary person must be involved when there is reason to believe that the primary person was involved in the alleged or suspected maltreatment.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The license holder shall:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) establish and maintain policies and procedures to ensure that an internal review is completed within 30 calendar days and that corrective action is taken as necessary to protect the health and safety of vulnerable adults when the facility has reason to know that an internal or external report of alleged or suspected maltreatment has been made. The review must include an evaluation of whether related policies and procedures were followed, whether the policies and procedures were adequate, whether there is a need for additional staff training, whether the reported event is similar to past events with the vulnerable adults or the services involved, and whether there is a need for corrective action by the license holder to protect the health and safety of vulnerable adults. Based on the results of this review, the license holder must develop, document, and implement a corrective action plan designed to correct current lapses and prevent future lapses in performance by individuals or the license holder, if any;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) identify the primary and secondary person or position who will ensure that, when required, internal reviews are completed. The secondary person shall be involved when there is reason to believe that the primary person was involved in the alleged or suspected maltreatment; and</p>
          <p class="in">(3) document and make internal reviews accessible to the commissioner immediately upon the commissioner's request. For the purposes of this section, the documentation provided to the commissioner by the license holder may consist of a completed checklist that verifies completion of each of the requirements of the review.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) The license holder shall provide an orientation to the internal and external reporting procedures to all persons receiving services. The orientation shall include the telephone number for the license holder's common entry point as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/626.5572#stat.626.5572.5">626.5572, subdivision 5</a>. If applicable, the person's legal representative must be notified of the orientation. The program shall provide this orientation for each new person within 24 hours of admission, or for persons who would benefit more from a later orientation, the orientation may take place within 72 hours.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) The license holder shall post a copy of the internal and external reporting policies and procedures, including the telephone number of the common entry point as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/626.5572#stat.626.5572.5">626.5572, subdivision 5</a>, in a prominent location in the program and have it available upon request to mandated reporters, persons receiving services, and the person's legal representatives.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.65.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Determination of vulnerable adult status.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) A license holder that provides services to adults who are excluded from the definition of vulnerable adult under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/626.5572#stat.626.5572.21">626.5572, subdivision 21</a>, paragraph (a), clause (2), must determine whether the person is a vulnerable adult under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/626.5572#stat.626.5572.21">626.5572, subdivision 21</a>, paragraph (a), clause (4). This determination must be made within 24 hours of:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) admission to the licensed program; and</p>
          <p class="in">(2) any incident that:</p>
          <p class="in">(i) was reported under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/626.557">626.557</a>; or</p>
          <p class="in">(ii) would have been required to be reported under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/626.557">626.557</a>, if one or more of the adults involved in the incident had been vulnerable adults.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Upon determining that a person receiving services is a vulnerable adult under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/626.5572#stat.626.5572.21">626.5572, subdivision 21</a>, paragraph (a), clause (4), all requirements relative to vulnerable adults under this chapter and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/626.557">626.557</a> must be met by the license holder.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.65.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Abuse prevention plans.</span></h2>
          <p>All license holders shall establish and enforce ongoing written program abuse prevention plans and individual abuse prevention plans as required under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/626.557#stat.626.557.14">626.557, subdivision 14</a>.</p>
          <p>(a) The scope of the program abuse prevention plan is limited to the population, physical plant, and environment within the control of the license holder and the location where licensed services are provided. In addition to the requirements in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/626.557#stat.626.557.14">626.557, subdivision 14</a>, the program abuse prevention plan shall meet the requirements in clauses (1) to (5).</p>
          <p>(1) The assessment of the population shall include an evaluation of the following factors: age, gender, mental functioning, physical and emotional health or behavior of the client; the need for specialized programs of care for clients; the need for training of staff to meet identified individual needs; and the knowledge a license holder may have regarding previous abuse that is relevant to minimizing risk of abuse for clients.</p>
          <p>(2) The assessment of the physical plant where the licensed services are provided shall include an evaluation of the following factors: the condition and design of the building as it relates to the safety of the clients; and the existence of areas in the building which are difficult to supervise.</p>
          <p>(3) The assessment of the environment for each facility and for each site when living arrangements are provided by the agency shall include an evaluation of the following factors: the location of the program in a particular neighborhood or community; the type of grounds and terrain surrounding the building; the type of internal programming; and the program's staffing patterns.</p>
          <p>(4) The license holder shall provide an orientation to the program abuse prevention plan for clients receiving services. If applicable, the client's legal representative must be notified of the orientation. The license holder shall provide this orientation for each new person within 24 hours of admission, or for persons who would benefit more from a later orientation, the orientation may take place within 72 hours.</p>
          <p>(5) The license holder's governing body or the governing body's delegated representative shall review the plan at least annually using the assessment factors in the plan and any substantiated maltreatment findings that occurred since the last review. The governing body or the governing body's delegated representative shall revise the plan, if necessary, to reflect the review results.</p>
          <p>(6) A copy of the program abuse prevention plan shall be posted in a prominent location in the program and be available upon request to mandated reporters, persons receiving services, and legal representatives.</p>
          <p>(b) In addition to the requirements in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/626.557#stat.626.557.14">626.557, subdivision 14</a>, the individual abuse prevention plan shall meet the requirements in clauses (1) and (2).</p>
          <p>(1) The plan shall include a statement of measures that will be taken to minimize the risk of abuse to the vulnerable adult when the individual assessment required in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/626.557#stat.626.557.14">626.557, subdivision 14</a>, paragraph (b), indicates the need for measures in addition to the specific measures identified in the program abuse prevention plan. The measures shall include the specific actions the program will take to minimize the risk of abuse within the scope of the licensed services, and will identify referrals made when the vulnerable adult is susceptible to abuse outside the scope or control of the licensed services. When the assessment indicates that the vulnerable adult does not need specific risk reduction measures in addition to those identified in the program abuse prevention plan, the individual abuse prevention plan shall document this determination.</p>
          <p>(2) An individual abuse prevention plan shall be developed for each new person as part of the initial individual program plan or service plan required under the applicable licensing rule or statute. The review and evaluation of the individual abuse prevention plan shall be done as part of the review of the program plan, service plan, or treatment plan. The person receiving services shall participate in the development of the individual abuse prevention plan to the full extent of the person's abilities. If applicable, the person's legal representative shall be given the opportunity to participate with or for the person in the development of the plan. The interdisciplinary team shall document the review of all abuse prevention plans at least annually, using the individual assessment and any reports of abuse relating to the person. The plan shall be revised to reflect the results of this review.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.65.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Orientation of mandated reporters.</span></h2>
          <p>The license holder shall ensure that each new mandated reporter, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/626.5572#stat.626.5572.16">626.5572, subdivision 16</a>, who is under the control of the license holder, receives an orientation within 72 hours of first providing direct contact services as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245C.02#stat.245C.02.11">245C.02, subdivision 11</a>, to a vulnerable adult and annually thereafter. The orientation and annual review shall inform the mandated reporters of the reporting requirements and definitions in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/626.557">626.557</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/626.5572">626.5572</a>, the requirements of this section, the license holder's program abuse prevention plan, and all internal policies and procedures related to the prevention and reporting of maltreatment of individuals receiving services.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245A.65.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/248/#laws.0.34.0">1997 c 248 s 34</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/15/#laws.1.33.0">2003 c 15 art 1 s 33</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/112/#laws.0.23.0">2007 c 112 s 23</a>,24; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/228/#laws.2.15.0">2014 c 228 art 2 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/71/#laws.7.7.0">2015 c 71 art 7 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.17.48.0">2021 c 30 art 17 s 48</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.245A.655"><b>245A.655</b> [Repealed, 2013 c 108 art 2 s 45]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">MALTREATMENT OF MINORS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245A.66">
        <h1 class="shn">245A.66 REQUIREMENTS; MALTREATMENT OF MINORS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.66.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Internal review.</span></h2>
          <p class="in"> License holders serving children are subject to the requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.54#stat.142B.54.1">142B.54, subdivision 1</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.66.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.54">142B.54, subd 2</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.66.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142B.54">142B.54, subd 3</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.66.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Ongoing training requirement.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) In addition to the orientation training required by the applicable licensing rules and statutes, children's residential facility license holders must provide a training annually on the maltreatment of minors reporting requirements and definitions in chapter 260E to each mandatory reporter, as described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260E.06#stat.260E.06.1">260E.06, subdivision 1</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) In addition to the orientation training required by the applicable licensing rules and statutes, all foster residence setting staff and volunteers that are mandatory reporters as described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260E.06#stat.260E.06.1">260E.06, subdivision 1</a>, must complete training each year on the maltreatment of minors reporting requirements and definitions in chapter 260E.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245A.66.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/112/#laws.0.25.0">2007 c 112 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/329/#laws.1.12.0">2010 c 329 art 1 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.16.14.0">2012 c 216 art 16 s 14</a>,15; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/228/#laws.2.16.0">2014 c 228 art 2 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/9/#laws.2.59.0">1Sp2019 c 9 art 2 s 59</a>,60; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.8.36.0">2023 c 70 art 8 s 36</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.2.72.0">2024 c 80 art 2 s 72</a>,73; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.13.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 13</a>; art 19 s 9</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245A.70">
        <h1 class="shn">245A.70 REMOTE ADULT DAY SERVICES.</h1>
        <p>(a) For the purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.70">245A.70</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.75">245A.75</a>, the following terms have the meanings given.</p>
        <p>(b) "Adult day care" and "adult day services" have the meanings given in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.02">245A.02</a>, subdivision 2a.</p>
        <p>(c) "Remote adult day services" means an individualized and coordinated set of services provided via live two-way communication by an adult day care or adult day services center.</p>
        <p>(d) "Live two-way communication" means real-time audio or audio and video transmission of information between a participant and an actively involved staff member.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245A.70.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/98/#laws.7.2.0">2022 c 98 art 7 s 2</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245A.71">
        <h1 class="shn">245A.71 APPLICABILITY AND SCOPE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.71.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Licensing requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>Adult day care centers or adult day services centers that provide remote adult day services must be licensed under this chapter and comply with the requirements set forth in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.71.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Standards for licensure.</span></h2>
          <p>License holders seeking to provide remote adult day services must submit a request in the manner prescribed by the commissioner. Remote adult day services must not be delivered until approved by the commissioner. The designation to provide remote services is voluntary for license holders. Upon approval, the designation of approval for remote adult day services must be printed on the center's license and identified on the commissioner's public website.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.71.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Federal requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>Adult day care centers or adult day services centers that provide remote adult day services to participants receiving alternative care under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0913">256B.0913</a>, essential community supports under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.0922">256B.0922</a>, or home and community-based services waivers under chapter 256S or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.092">256B.092</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.49">256B.49 </a>must comply with federally approved waiver plans.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.71.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Service limitations.</span></h2>
          <p>Remote adult day services must be provided during the days and hours of in-person services specified on the license of the adult day care center or adult day services center.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245A.71.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/98/#laws.7.3.0">2022 c 98 art 7 s 3</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245A.72">
        <h1 class="shn">245A.72 RECORD REQUIREMENTS.</h1>
        <p>Adult day care centers and adult day services centers providing remote adult day services must comply with participant record requirements set forth in Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9555.9660">9555.9660</a>. The center must document how remote services will help a participant reach the short- and long-term objectives in the participant's plan of care.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245A.72.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/98/#laws.7.4.0">2022 c 98 art 7 s 4</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245A.73">
        <h1 class="shn">245A.73 REMOTE ADULT DAY SERVICES STAFF.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.73.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Staff ratios.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A staff person who provides remote adult day services without two-way interactive video must only provide services to one participant at a time.</p>
          <p>(b) A staff person who provides remote adult day services through two-way interactive video must not provide services to more than eight participants at one time.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.73.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Staff training.</span></h2>
          <p>A center licensed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245A.71">245A.71</a> must document training provided to each staff person regarding the provision of remote services in the staff person's record. The training must be provided prior to a staff person delivering remote adult day services without supervision. The training must include:</p>
          <p>(1) how to use the equipment, technology, and devices required to provide remote adult day services via live two-way communication;</p>
          <p>(2) orientation and training on each participant's plan of care as directly related to remote adult day services; and</p>
          <p>(3) direct observation by a manager or supervisor of the staff person while providing supervised remote service delivery sufficient to assess staff competency.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245A.73.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/98/#laws.7.5.0">2022 c 98 art 7 s 5</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245A.74">
        <h1 class="shn">245A.74 INDIVIDUAL SERVICE PLANNING.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.74.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Eligibility.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A person must be eligible for and receiving in-person adult day services to receive remote adult day services from the same provider. The same provider must deliver both in-person adult day services and remote adult day services to a participant.</p>
          <p>(b) The license holder must update the participant's plan of care according to Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9555.9700">9555.9700</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) For a participant who chooses to receive remote adult day services, the license holder must document in the participant's plan of care the participant's proposed schedule and frequency for receiving both in-person and remote services. The license holder must also document in the participant's plan of care that remote services:</p>
          <p>(1) are chosen as a service delivery method by the participant or the participant's legal representative;</p>
          <p>(2) will meet the participant's assessed needs;</p>
          <p>(3) are provided within the scope of adult day services; and</p>
          <p>(4) will help the participant achieve identified short- and long-term objectives specific to the provision of remote adult day services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.74.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Participant daily service limitations.</span></h2>
          <p>In a 24-hour period, a participant may receive:</p>
          <p>(1) a combination of in-person adult day services and remote adult day services on the same day but not at the same time;</p>
          <p>(2) a combination of in-person and remote adult day services that does not exceed 12 hours in total; and</p>
          <p>(3) up to six hours of remote adult day services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.245A.74.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Minimum in-person requirement.</span></h2>
          <p>A participant who receives remote services must receive services in-person as assigned in the participant's plan of care at least quarterly.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245A.74.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/98/#laws.7.6.0">2022 c 98 art 7 s 6</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.245A.75">
        <h1 class="shn">245A.75 SERVICE AND PROGRAM REQUIREMENTS.</h1>
        <p>Remote adult day services must be in the scope of adult day services provided in Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/9555.9710">9555.9710</a>, subparts 3 to 7.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.245A.75.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/98/#laws.7.7.0">2022 c 98 art 7 s 7</a>
        </p>
      </div>
    </div>
